```rust
pub enum Value { BaseUrl(&'static str /* provider */, &'static str /* suffix */), Placeholder(&'static str /* cred */) }
pub struct Adapter { pub harness: &'static str, pub creds: &'static [(&'static str, &'static str)], pub env: &'static [EnvVar] }
```

## P9: agent safety extras (first three)

### T9.02 Prompt DLP: block requests containing a stored secret (M) 🔒

**Depends on:** T1.10.

**Files:** create `crates/kepr-gate/src/dlp.rs`; call it in `proxy.rs`
before forwarding.

- Keep the set of values this gate session has resolved (from the T1.09
  cache). Before forwarding, buffer the request body up to 8 MiB (larger →
  `413`), and check whether any cached value appears in it as bytes
  (`memchr::memmem` is acceptable; add `memchr` to the gate crate). Also
  check for these patterns: `sk-ant-[A-Za-z0-9_-]{20,}`, `xai-[A-Za-z0-9]{20,}`,
  `AKIA[0-9A-Z]{16}`, `ghp_[A-Za-z0-9]{36}`, `glpat-[A-Za-z0-9_-]{20,}`
  (hand-written matchers, no regex crate).
- On a match, do not forward. Respond `403` with body
  `{"error":{"type":"kepr_blocked","message":"kepr blocked this request: it contains the stored secret NAME. Remove it and retry."}}`.
  For pattern matches say `"it contains what looks like an API key"`.
  Never put the matched bytes in the response or the journal.
- Journal the block with `"status":403,"blocked":"dlp"`.

**Tests:** body containing a cached value → 403 and upstream untouched;
body with `AKIA` + 16 chars → 403; clean body → forwarded; the 403 body
contains the credential NAME but not the value.

### T9.03 Output masking for `kepr exec --mask` (M)

**Depends on:** nothing.

**Files:** create `src/cli_mask.rs`; add `--mask` to `src/cli_args.rs`;
use it in `src/cli_exec.rs`.

With `--mask`, `kepr exec` can't use `execvp` (it must stay alive to filter
output). Spawn the child with piped stdout/stderr, copy each stream through
a `Masker` to our own stdout/stderr, and exit with the child's exit code.

```rust
pub struct Masker { /* secrets, carry-over buffer */ }
impl Masker {
    pub fn new(secrets: Vec<ZeroString>) -> Self;
    /// Returns bytes safe to emit now; keeps up to (longest secret - 1)
    /// bytes back so a secret split across two reads is still caught.
    pub fn feed(&mut self, chunk: &[u8]) -> Vec<u8>;
    pub fn finish(&mut self) -> Vec<u8>;
}
```

Each occurrence of a secret becomes `****`.

**Tests:** secret in one chunk; secret split across two chunks; two
secrets; no secret (output identical); `finish` flushes the held-back tail.

### T9.01 Per-agent approvals (L)

Split first, following plan P9.1.

## P3: secret enclave in RAM

### T3.01 `secmem` capability probe (S)

**Files:** create `crates/kepr-core/src/secmem.rs` with
`pub fn memfd_secret_available() -> bool` (call
`libc::syscall(libc::SYS_memfd_secret, 0)`; success → close the fd and
return true). Test: the function returns without panicking; on Linux it
returns a bool consistent with a second call.

### T3.02 `SecretRegion` allocator (M) 🔒

**Depends on:** T3.01. A type that owns one page-aligned region: backed by
`memfd_secret` + `mmap` when available, otherwise anonymous `mmap` +
`mlock` + `madvise(MADV_DONTDUMP | MADV_WIPEONFORK)`. One `PROT_NONE` guard
page before and after. Placement at a random page-aligned hint address
(`getrandom`), retrying without the hint if `mmap` refuses. `Drop` zeroizes,
`munlock`s and `munmap`s. API: `new(len) -> Result<Self, String>`,
`as_slice()`, `as_mut_slice()`. Tests: write/read round-trip, length
rounding, drop doesn't crash, guard-page layout (inspect
`/proc/self/maps`).

### T3.03 Relocation and XOR splitting (M) 🔒

**Depends on:** T3.02. `SplitKey` holding two `SecretRegion`s A and
B = key ⊕ A; `with_key(|k| ...)` recombines into a stack buffer and
zeroizes it after the closure; `relocate()` allocates fresh regions with a
new random A and drops the old ones. Tests: `with_key` sees the original
key before and after `relocate`; the raw bytes of neither region equal the
key.

### T3.04 `kepr doctor` hardening section (M)

Add checks (each a pure function over file contents, plus one thin I/O
wrapper) for: `memfd_secret` (T3.01), Yama `ptrace_scope`, `lockdown`,
swap on/encrypted (`/proc/swaps`), Landlock ABI, unprivileged userns
(`/proc/sys/kernel/unprivileged_userns_clone` and the AppArmor restriction
sysctl), TME/SME flags in `/proc/cpuinfo`, IOMMU groups present. Follow the
existing structure of `src/cli_doctor.rs`.

### T3.05 Lock on suspend and `kepr panic` (L)

Split first, following plan P3.5.

## Open work, split into tickets (2026-10-05)

Everything the plan still lists as open, plus what the 2026-10-05 Keeper
re-check found (plan section 2.7, phase P11), split into S/M tickets with
the template at the bottom of this page. Nothing here talks to Keeper: every
feature is built into kepr.

**Shared wiring.** Unless a ticket says otherwise:

- A new daemon request is a variant in `crates/kepr-core/src/protocol.rs`
  (response variants in `protocol_response.rs`), routed from
  `src/daemon_extra.rs::dispatch` to a handler in a new `src/daemon_*.rs`
  file that calls `audit::log` with op and name only.
- A new CLI verb is added to `VERBS` and `dispatch` in `src/cli_extra.rs`
  (or a new `src/cli_*.rs` with its own small parser) and to the help text in
  `src/cli_help.rs`.
- New `.meta` fields go on `CredMeta` in `src/expiry.rs` with
  `#[serde(skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none", default)]`, so older
  daemons and older files keep working.
- "Done when" is always the four standard commands from *How to work a
  ticket*, plus anything extra the ticket names.

## P1 (remaining)

### T1.23 Landlock rules in the agent sandbox (M) 🔒

*Shipped in b807afd and merge 46a7d6a; differs: see status.*

Goal: on kernels with Landlock, the harness cannot open the vault, the
daemon socket or the gate's files even if a hiding mount is undone.
Depends on: nothing (T1.18 shipped).
Files: create `src/cli_sandbox_landlock.rs`; call it from
`src/cli_agent_sandbox.rs` in the child after the mounts, before `execvp`.
Add: `pub fn restrict(deny: &[PathBuf]) -> Result<LandlockStatus, String>`
using raw syscalls (`landlock_create_ruleset`, `landlock_add_rule`,
`landlock_restrict_self` via `libc::syscall`; no new crate). Rule set:
handle all filesystem access rights of the running ABI; allow read+exec on
`/`, read+write on `$HOME`, `/tmp`, `/dev`, `/proc`, `$PWD`; leave the deny
paths (store dir, `$XDG_RUNTIME_DIR/kepr`, `/run/kepr`) without a rule
**below** an allowed parent by opening the allowed parents one level at a
time (Landlock is allow-only, so `$HOME` is granted per child entry, skipping
`~/.local/share/kepr` and `~/.local/state/kepr`). ABI below 1 → `Unsupported`
(not an error; the mounts still apply).
Tests: `ruleset_attr_for_abi` returns the documented access masks for ABI
1, 2, 3; `children_except` lists a temp dir's entries minus the denied one;
`restrict` in a forked child on a Landlock kernel makes opening a denied
temp file fail with `EACCES` (skip when the probe says unsupported).
Out of scope: network rules (Landlock ABI 4); the net namespace already
handles those.

### T2.01 Move the SigV4 primitives into kepr-core (S)

*Shipped in 517f8c3.*

Goal: the gate can sign AWS requests with the same pinned code as `sync`.
Depends on: nothing.
Files: move `src/sync_aws_sigv4.rs` to `crates/kepr-core/src/sigv4.rs`
(`pub mod sigv4` in `lib.rs`); `src/sync_aws_sm.rs` imports it from there;
add `sha2` and `hmac` to `kepr-core`'s `Cargo.toml` (already in the tree).
Tests: the existing AWS test vectors move with the file and still pass.
Out of scope: any behaviour change.

### T2.02 Gate SigV4 verify-and-re-sign (M) 🔒

*Shipped in ec5e181; differs: see status.*

Goal: `aws` CLI and SDKs inside an agent session sign requests with a
placeholder secret; the gate checks that signature and re-signs with the
real key, only toward a bound `*.amazonaws.com` host.
Depends on: T2.01.
Files: `crates/kepr-core/src/binding.rs` (new `Inject::Sigv4`, value stored
as JSON `{"access_key_id","secret_access_key","session_token"?}`);
create `crates/kepr-gate/src/sigv4_resign.rs`; call it from
`crates/kepr-gate/src/egress.rs` for routes whose binding is `Sigv4`.
Add: `parse_authorization(h) -> Option<SigV4Auth>`;
`verify(req, placeholder_secret) -> bool` (recompute with the placeholder
secret, constant-time compare); `resign(req, real) -> HeaderMap` (new
`x-amz-date`, `Authorization`, `x-amz-security-token` when present; drops
the placeholder's). The placeholder access key id is `KEPRPH` + 14 base32
chars so it is shaped like an AWS key id.
Tests: a request signed with the placeholder verifies; tampering with the
path or a signed header fails verification; `resign` output verifies under
the real secret (reuse the AWS test vector date and region); unsigned
payload (`UNSIGNED-PAYLOAD`) is supported; a non-`amazonaws.com` host is
refused at binding validation.
Out of scope: SigV4a, presigned URLs (refuse them with 403).

### T2.03 `kepr aws credential-process` (M) 🔒

*Shipped in 5617576; differs: see status.*

Goal: `~/.aws/config` can say `credential_process = kepr aws
credential-process NAME` and get short-lived credentials, never the
long-lived key.
Depends on: T7.06 (STS client).
Files: create `src/cli_aws.rs` (verb `aws`); reuse `src/sts.rs` from T7.06.
Behaviour: inside an agent session (`KEPR_SESSION` set by `kepr agent run`)
print the session's SigV4 placeholder pair (T2.02) in the
`credential_process` JSON shape (`Version: 1`, `AccessKeyId`,
`SecretAccessKey`, `Expiration`); outside a session call STS
`GetSessionToken` (900–3600 s, `--duration`) with the stored key and print
the temporary credentials. A sealed key is never printed in either case.
Tests: JSON shape matches the AWS spec; inside-session path never calls the
daemon `Get`; STS XML response parsing (fixture) extracts the three values
and the expiry; refuses a duration outside 900..=43200.

### T2.04 Placeholder presets for common CLIs (M)

*Shipped in 26ed7ec; differs: see status.*

Goal: `gh`, `git`, `aws`, `gcloud`, `stripe`, `npm` and `docker` work inside
an agent session with no manual setup.
Depends on: nothing.

(`api.stripe.com`, Bearer, `STRIPE_API_KEY`, prefix `sk_live_`/`sk_test_`),

and a `docker` `config.json` with a placeholder `auth`) into the session's

with an npm key sets `NPM_CONFIG_USERCONFIG`.

## P3 (remaining)

### T3.06 Pull-on-demand fd delivery (M) 🔒

*Shipped in 082d490 and merge 46a7d6a; differs: see status.*

Goal: a cooperating process fetches a secret as a sealed fd over the daemon
socket instead of inheriting one, so nothing leaks to its children.
Depends on: nothing.
Files: protocol `Request::FetchFd { name }` / `Response::FdOk { len }`;
create `src/daemon_fetchfd.rs` (after the normal `Get` policy checks, create
a sealed memfd with `FD_CLOEXEC` **set** and send it with
`kepr_core::fdpass::send_fd` after the `FdOk` frame); client helper
`kepr_core::fdfetch::fetch(name) -> Result<OwnedFd, String>`;
`kepr exec --pull NAME` sets `KEPR_PULL=NAME,…` instead of passing fds.
SDKs get `fetch_fd` in T8.09.
Tests: the received fd has `FD_CLOEXEC` set and is sealed
(`F_GET_SEALS` includes `F_SEAL_WRITE`); a sealed / ssh / share-key
credential is refused exactly like `Get`; a child spawned after the fetch
cannot read `/proc/self/fd/N` for it.

## P4: vault image and hardware-bound keys

The image is an optional second store (`KEPR_STORE=image`). The per-file
store stays the default (decision 4).

### T4.01 Vault image: header, slots and noise fill (M) 🔒

*Shipped in 4b95c1c; differs: see status.*

Goal: `VaultImage::create(path, size)` writes a fixed-size file of CSPRNG
noise with an authenticated header, in 4 KiB slots.
Depends on: nothing.
Files: create `src/vault_image.rs` (daemon only; `mod` it from
`src/daemon.rs`).
Add: header in slot 0 = magic `kepr_vi1`, version, image id (16 random
bytes), slot count, epoch (u64), KDF salt, Merkle root (32 bytes), header
MAC. `create(path, size_mib)` uses `fallocate` (fallback: write noise in
1 MiB chunks), `O_EXCL`, mode 0600, fsync. `open(path)` reads the header and
checks magic and size. Size must be a multiple of 1 MiB, 1..=4096 MiB.
Tests: created file has exactly the requested size; two images differ
everywhere except the magic; `open` refuses a wrong magic, a truncated file
and a size that doesn't match the slot count.

### T4.02 Per-slot AEAD and the Merkle root (M) 🔒

*Shipped in ac82d13; differs: see status.*

Goal: each slot is sealed with AES-256-GCM under AAD = `image_id ‖ slot ‖
epoch`; a Merkle tree over slot ciphertext hashes is stored in the header.
Depends on: T4.01.
Files: `src/vault_image.rs`; create `src/vault_merkle.rs`.
Add: `seal_slot(key, idx, epoch, plaintext<=4064) -> [u8; 4096]`,
`open_slot(...)`; `merkle_root(hashes)` (SHA-256, duplicate last on odd
levels); `VaultImage::verify()` recomputes the root.
Tests: moving a sealed slot to another index fails to open; flipping one
ciphertext bit changes the root and `verify` reports the slot; a slot from
an older epoch fails.

### T4.03 Slot placement and decoy rewrites (S)

*Shipped in cf9d996; differs: see status.*

Goal: a record's slot index is `HMAC(slot_key, record_id ‖ attempt) mod N`
with probing; every write also rewrites k random decoy slots with fresh
noise.
Depends on: T4.02.
Files: `src/vault_image.rs` (or `src/vault_place.rs` if over the limit).
Tests: placement is deterministic for a key; two records never share a slot
(probing); a write changes exactly 1 + k slots (k = 3).

### T4.04 Journal slot and two-phase commit (M) 🔒

*Shipped in cad1323; differs: see status.*

Goal: a crash at any point leaves either the old or the new image state.
Depends on: T4.03.
Files: `src/vault_image.rs` / `src/vault_journal.rs`.
Add: slot 1 is the journal: write new slots' (index, ciphertext) list
sealed under the next epoch, fsync, then the slots, fsync, then the header
with the new epoch and root, fsync, then clear the journal. `open` replays a
complete journal and discards a torn one.
Tests (fault injection through a `Writer` trait that fails after N bytes):
for every N, reopening yields the old or the new state, never a mix, and
`verify` passes.

### T4.05 Record keys and crypto-shred delete (M) 🔒

*Shipped in ba7f21e; differs: see status.*

Goal: every record has its own random AES-256 key wrapped under the vault
master key; delete destroys the wrapped key and bumps the epoch.
Depends on: T4.04.
Files: `src/vault_image.rs`, create `src/vault_records.rs`.
Add: an index record (name → record id, wrapped record key, slot list)
sealed under `index_key = HKDF(vmk, "index")`; record payload = value +
`.meta` JSON sealed under its record key.
Tests: deleting a record makes its old slot bytes undecryptable even with
the VMK (key gone); renaming the index entry to another name fails AAD.

### T4.06 Image as a credential backend (M) 🔒

*Shipped in 7902d87; differs: see status.*

Goal: with `KEPR_STORE=image` the daemon stores credentials and metadata in
the image instead of per-file.
Depends on: T4.05.
Files: create `src/crypto_image.rs` implementing `crypto::Backend`; meta
read/write in `src/expiry.rs` go through a small `MetaStore` trait with the
file implementation as today and an image one; `src/daemon.rs` start-up
picks the backend.
Tests: the backend passes the same round-trip / wrong-uid / wrong-name tests
as `crypto_inproc`; set → list → delete through the handlers works with the
image backend in a temp dir.

### T4.07 `kepr vault init | migrate | verify | status` (M)

*Shipped in a10a33a; differs: see status.*

Goal: users can create an image, move the per-file store into it, and check
it.
Depends on: T4.06.
Files: protocol `VaultInit { size_mib }`, `VaultMigrate`, `VaultVerify`,
`VaultStatus`; create `src/daemon_vault.rs`, `src/cli_vault.rs`.
Behaviour: `migrate` copies every credential (with meta and history),
verifies each by decrypting from the image, then deletes the per-file
copies; it refuses while a gate runs. `status` prints backend, size, used
slots (approximate: never exact counts to non-owners), epoch.
Tests: migrate of three creds (one sealed, one with history) round-trips;
verify after a bit flip fails naming the slot.

### T4.08 TPM2 PIN and anti-rollback counter (M) 🔒

*Shipped in c25b57c; differs: see status.*

Goal: the TPM2 seal can require a PIN, and an NV counter rejects an older
copy of the sealed master key or vault image.
Depends on: nothing (TPM2 sealing shipped).
Files: `src/master_key.rs`, create `src/master_key_tpm.rs`.
Add: `KEPR_TPM2_PIN=1` seals with `systemd-creds --tpm2-with-pin`-equivalent
(`tpm2_create` policy `PolicyAuthValue`, through `tpm2-tools` when present);
`KEPR_TPM2_NV_INDEX` (default `0x01800A11`) is created with
`tpm2_nvdefine -a "nt=counter"` on first seal, incremented on every master-
key change, and its value is written next to the sealed blob; unseal refuses
when the stored value is lower than the TPM's.
Tests (a `TpmRunner` trait with a fake): first seal defines the index;
rotation increments; an old blob with a lower counter is refused; a missing
TPM tool gives a clear error naming the package `tpm2-tools`.

### T4.09 FIDO2 `hmac-secret` second factor for the master key (M) 🔒

*Shipped in 33a80bf; differs: see status.*

Goal: unlocking needs the passphrase **and** a touch of a registered
security key.
Depends on: nothing.
Files: `src/key_unlock.rs`, create `src/key_fido2.rs`.
Add: `kepr lock --fido2` runs `fido2-cred -M -h` to make a credential with
`hmac-secret`, stores the credential id and salt next to the wrapped key;
the KEK becomes `HKDF(argon2id(passphrase) ‖ hmac_secret_output)`;
`kepr unlock` runs `fido2-assert -G -h` with the stored salt. Tool access
goes through a runner trait.
Tests: KEK derivation is deterministic and differs from passphrase-only;
unlock without the factor fails; parsing of `fido2-assert` output (fixture).

### T4.10 Recovery key: words and a terminal QR code (S)

*Shipped in 4a883ad; differs: see status.*

Goal: `kepr recovery-kit create --words` prints a 24-word key (EFF list,
~310 bits) and a QR code of it on the terminal.
Depends on: nothing.
Files: `src/cli_recovery.rs`, create `src/cli_qr.rs`; add `qrcode = { version
= "0.14", default-features = false }`.
Tests: words ↔ bytes round trip; the QR renders with half-block characters
and a quiet zone; a wrong word reports its position.

### T4.11 Bring your own key: sealer trait and AWS KMS (M) 🔒

*Shipped in de04857; differs: see status.*

Goal: `KEPR_MASTER_KEY_SEAL=aws-kms:<key-arn>` wraps the master key with
AWS KMS `Encrypt` / `Decrypt` (SigV4, T2.01).
Depends on: T2.01.
Files: create `src/master_key_kms.rs` with `trait KekSealer { seal, unseal }`
and `AwsKms`; `src/master_key.rs` dispatches on the prefix.
Tests: request bodies and headers for Encrypt/Decrypt match the AWS JSON
1.1 shape (`X-Amz-Target: TrentService.Encrypt`); response parsing; an
encryption context `{"kepr":"master-key"}` is always sent.

### T4.12 GCP KMS and Azure Key Vault sealers (M) 🔒

*Shipped in f862ad3; differs: see status.*

Goal: `gcp-kms:projects/…/cryptoKeys/…` and
`azure-kv:https://NAME.vault.azure.net/keys/KEY/VERSION` sealers.
Depends on: T4.11.
Files: `src/master_key_kms.rs` (or `master_key_kms_cloud.rs`).
Auth: `GCP_ACCESS_TOKEN`, `AZURE_ACCESS_TOKEN` (bearer, like `sync`).
Tests: request JSON and URLs for encrypt/decrypt (GCP `:encrypt`,
Azure `/wrapkey` with `RSA-OAEP-256`), response parsing, base64url handling.

### T4.13 PKCS#11 / YubiHSM sealer (S) 🔒

*Shipped in 75de4a8; differs: see status.*

Goal: `pkcs11:<module>:<key-label>` wraps the master key through
`pkcs11-tool --encrypt/--decrypt` (RSA-OAEP).
Depends on: T4.11.
Tests: command lines built correctly (fake runner); PIN read from

### T4.14 Every store feature on the image backend (M) 🔒

*Shipped in 5b56f78, 6d7454d and 79322f8; differs: see status.*

Goal: trash, aliases, folders/tags, duplicates, `delete --all`, history

(aliases) are allowed in the image.
Tests: `tests/vault_image.rs` runs set → rm (to trash) → trash restore,
alias resolve, `delete --all` and `doctor --verify` against a daemon using
the image.

### T5.01 Tags and folders (M)

--folder f`.

`mv NAME /` clears the folder; history slots and sealed creds keep their
tags across `set`.

### T5.02 Per-project environments in `.kepr.toml` (S)

*Shipped in 45408f1; differs: see status.*

Goal: one manifest serves several environments.
Depends on: nothing.
Files: `src/cli_manifest.rs`, `src/cli_exec.rs`.
Schema: `[env] required = [...]` stays; new `[env.<stage>] required = [...]`
and `[env.<stage>.map] VAR = "kepr://NAME/field"`. `kepr exec --stage dev`
picks one (default stage from `KEPR_STAGE`).

resolver.

### T5.03 Attachments (M) 🔒

*Shipped in bc34e03; differs: see status.*

Goal: `kepr attach add NAME FILE`, `attach get NAME FILE -o OUT`,
`attach ls NAME`, `attach rm NAME FILE`; up to 16 MiB per file, 64 MiB per
record.
Depends on: nothing.
Files: protocol `AttachPut { name, file, chunk, index, last }`,
`AttachGet { name, file, index }`, `AttachList`, `AttachRemove`; create
`src/daemon_attach.rs`, `src/cli_attach.rs`.
Storage: chunks of 48 KiB as credentials named `NAME~a<file-id>~<n>` (the
`~` keeps them out of listings, like history), a manifest in `.meta`
`attachments: [{id, file, size, sha256}]`. Sealed records refuse
attachments. Delete of the record removes them. `get` writes with mode 0600
and refuses to overwrite without `--force`.
Tests: 200 KiB file round-trips byte-exact; sha256 mismatch on a tampered
chunk is reported; cap enforced; names with `/` or `..` refused.

### T5.04 Generator profiles and a strength estimate (S)

Depends on: nothing.

`src/cli_args.rs` (`--profile`).
Profiles can be keyed by host (`[host."example.com"]`) and by record type.
Strength = min(charset entropy, pattern penalties for repeats, sequences,
dictionary words from the EFF list, years).

`Password1` scores below 30 bits, a 6-word passphrase above 70.

record with array fields and a custom field containing `/`; `file/` returns

## P6: sharing, recovery, hygiene, sync, policy

### T6.01 ML-KEM-768 hybrid share envelopes (M) 🔒

*Shipped in 4944012; differs: see status.*

Goal: share envelopes resist a future quantum attacker: the content key is
derived from both an X25519 and an ML-KEM-768 shared secret.
Depends on: nothing (P6.1/P6.2 shipped).
Files: `src/daemon_share.rs` (or a new `src/share_kem.rs`); add `ml-kem`
(RustCrypto) to the root crate.
Add: the share key record stores both secret keys; `kepr share key` prints
`kepr-pk2-<base64(x25519_pub ‖ mlkem_ek)>`; envelope v2 = ephemeral X25519
pub ‖ ML-KEM ciphertext ‖ AES-GCM body; key = `HKDF-SHA256(x25519_ss ‖
mlkem_ss, info="kepr-share-v2" ‖ both public keys)`. v1 envelopes and v1
public keys keep working (receive both; send v2 only to v2 keys).
Tests: v2 round trip; v1 still received; a v2 envelope with the ML-KEM
ciphertext swapped fails; a v1-only recipient gets v1.

### T6.02 Shared folders (M) 🔒

*Shipped in de14433; differs: see status.*

Goal: `kepr folder share FOLDER --to KEY…` makes a folder bundle other
users can open with their share keys, on any shared path (a git repo, a
network drive).
Depends on: T6.01, T5.01.
Files: create `src/daemon_folder.rs`, `src/cli_folder.rs`; protocol
`FolderExport { folder, recipients }`, `FolderImport { bundle }`.
Format: one random folder key; each credential in the folder sealed under
it (AES-256-GCM, AAD = folder id ‖ name); the folder key wrapped per
recipient with the v2 envelope; a signed manifest (Ed25519 key of the
exporter, stored like an SSH key). Import verifies the signature against a
pinned contact (T11.15) and writes credentials as plain or sealed per the
bundle flag. Sealed / ssh / share-key values never go into a bundle.
Tests: export → import round trip for two recipients; a third key can't
open it; a modified bundle fails the signature; re-import updates only
changed values.

### T6.03 Devices and single-use enrolment tokens (M) 🔒

*Shipped in baf8e13; differs: see status.*

Goal: `kepr device add LABEL` prints a single-use token (`KD1-…`, 15 min)
for another machine; `kepr device enroll TOKEN --from PATH` on the new
machine registers its share key with the first one; `kepr device ls|rm`.
Depends on: T6.01.
Files: create `src/daemon_device.rs`, `src/cli_device.rs`; protocol
`DeviceAdd`, `DeviceEnroll`, `DeviceList`, `DeviceRemove`.
The token is a random 128-bit secret; the daemon stores only its SHA-256 and
expiry. Enrolment exchanges a file (or a relay blob, T6.04) carrying the
new device's public key and an HMAC under the token; the first device pins
it. Devices are what sync (T6.10) encrypts to.
Tests: token works once; expired token refused; HMAC mismatch refused;
`rm` drops the device from future sync bundles.

### T6.04 One-time share links and a relay (M) 🔒

*Shipped in 1b6a19c; differs: see status.*

Goal: `kepr share send NAME --once [--ttl 1h] [--relay URL]` writes a
single-use blob and prints `kepr://share/<id>#<key>` (key only in the
fragment), plus a QR code with `--qr`; `kepr share open LINK` fetches and
decrypts. `kepr-relay` is a tiny optional HTTP server that stores blobs and
deletes each on first fetch or expiry.
Depends on: nothing (T4.10 for the QR helper, or inline it).
Files: create `src/cli_share_once.rs`; create `crates/kepr-relay/`
(std `TcpListener`, no async runtime, 1 MiB blob cap, `PUT /b/<id>`,
`GET /b/<id>` = fetch-and-delete, per-IP rate limit). Without `--relay`
the blob is a local file to pass on by other means.
Tests: link → open round trip through a relay on 127.0.0.1; second open
fails; the relay never receives the key (assert on the request bytes);
expired blob gone.

### T6.05 Self-destructing records (S)

*Shipped in f64d566; differs: see status.*

Goal: `kepr share send NAME --once --self-destruct` deletes the record
(and its history) once the share is opened or expires.
Depends on: T6.04.
Files: `src/daemon_share.rs`, `src/daemon_expiry.rs` (sweeper checks a
`self_destruct_share` meta field).
Tests: after open (simulated by the receive path) the record is gone;
after expiry the sweeper deletes it; audit shows `self-destruct`.

### T6.06 Emergency access with a time delay (M) 🔒

*Shipped in 1caf662; differs: see status.*

Goal: `kepr emergency add CONTACT_KEY --delay 7d` lets a contact request
access; if the owner doesn't deny within the delay, the daemon releases an
envelope of the chosen records to them.
Depends on: T6.01.
Files: create `src/daemon_emergency.rs`, `src/cli_emergency.rs`; protocol
`EmergencyAdd`, `EmergencyList`, `EmergencyRequest { from, proof }`,
`EmergencyDeny`, `EmergencyRelease`.
The request is a file or relay blob signed by the contact's key. The sweeper
(`daemon_expiry.rs`) releases when `now ≥ requested_at + delay` and no
denial; release writes envelopes (never sealed creds) and audits loudly.
Max delay 90 days, up to 5 contacts (Keeper's limits).
Tests: request then wait (injected clock) releases; deny cancels; a forged
request (wrong key) is refused; sealed creds are never included.

### T6.07 Break-glass reveal of a sealed credential (M) 🔒

*Shipped in 8a2b3e7 (fix in 971dbfc).*

Goal: the only way to read a sealed value back: present k recovery shares
(P6.3), the value is printed once, the event is audited as `break-glass`,
and the credential is marked `must_rotate`.
Depends on: nothing (recovery kit shipped).
Files: create `src/daemon_breakglass.rs`; protocol `BreakGlass { name,
shares }`; `kepr break-glass NAME`; `AGENTS.md` documents it next to the
recovery-kit exception.
Rationale: anyone holding k shares can already rebuild the master key and
decrypt the files offline, so this adds no new capability; it makes the
access loud instead. Refused while any gate runs.
Tests: correct shares reveal and set `must_rotate`; wrong shares refused
without revealing which share was wrong; `kepr list` flags `must_rotate`;
the next `set` clears it.

### T6.08 Webhook alerts for health and breach findings (S)

*Shipped in dfeac70; differs: see status.*

Goal: `kepr health --breach --notify URL` posts a JSON summary (names and
flags only) to a webhook (Slack / Teams / Discord / ntfy formats picked by
`--format`).
Depends on: nothing.
Files: create `src/notify.rs` (shared by T7.15, T9.04); `src/cli_health.rs`.
Tests: body for each format; no value or hash prefix ever in the body;
non-2xx reported.

### T6.09 Encrypted bundle export and import (M) 🔒

*Shipped in 47f4733; differs: see status.*

Goal: `kepr export --encrypted -o FILE` writes every non-sealed credential
(values, meta, history, attachments) to a passphrase-protected bundle;
`kepr import FILE` detects and opens it.
Depends on: nothing.
Files: create `src/bundle.rs` (format shared with sync, T6.10),
`src/daemon_export.rs`; protocol `ExportBundle { passphrase }` returning
the bundle in chunks.
Format: `kepr_bundle1` magic, Argon2id params + salt, AES-256-GCM over a
JSON body; sealed / ssh / share-key items are listed by name with
`"omitted":"sealed"`.
Tests: round trip; wrong passphrase refused; sealed values absent from the
plaintext body; import reports omitted items.

### T6.10 Multi-device sync (M)

*Shipped in c91c3b9; differs: see status.*

Goal: `kepr sync remote add dir:PATH|git:URL|s3:BUCKET/PREFIX`, `kepr sync
now`: each device pushes a bundle encrypted to every enrolled device's key
(T6.03) and merges others' bundles last-writer-wins per credential.
Depends on: T6.09, T6.03.
Files: create `src/sync_devices.rs`, `src/sync_transport.rs` (dir, git via
`git` CLI, S3 via SigV4 from T2.01).
Merge: each credential carries `updated_at` and `origin`; ties broken by
device id; deletes are tombstones kept 30 days.
Tests: two simulated devices converge; delete propagates; a bundle from an
unknown device is ignored; a bundle encrypted to a removed device can't be
opened by it after re-key.

*Shipped in 2a6f0b3; differs: see status.*

credentials may be sealed to which hosts.
Depends on: nothing.
Files: create `src/policy.rs` (parser + evaluator, pure), `src/cli_policy.rs`;
the daemon loads `~/.config/kepr/policy.toml` (or `/etc/kepr/policy.toml`
in system mode) at start and on `SIGHUP`; handlers call
`policy::check(op, ctx)`.
`kepr policy test FILE` runs `[[test]]` cases in the file (`op`, `name`,
`exe`, expected `allow|deny`).
Tests: deny by default when the file exists; first-match rules; glob names;
test cases pass/fail with readable output; a missing file means "no policy"
(today's behaviour).

## P12: plan gaps (2026-10-15 audit)

On 2026-10-15 every promise in the [plan](/docs/agent-vault-plan) was
checked against the code. Where the code made a sound, deliberate choice
(HTTP/1.1 only in the gate, for example), the plan text was corrected
instead. The tickets below cover the promises the code should keep. Three
drafts were dropped as duplicates: a second 64 MiB default ticket, a second
security-key removal watcher, and a doctor-only AppArmor check that T12.34
covers.

Order: the groups touch different files and can be built in parallel.
Inside a group, follow the numbers wherever a ticket names a dependency.
T12.40 lands after T12.27 and T12.28, because all three change the vault
image's crypto.

**Gate and agent sessions**

### T12.01 Mask resolved secrets in gate responses (M) 🔒

Goal: no HTTP response the gate hands an agent (base-URL, egress or AWS egress) contains a value the session resolved. Each occurrence becomes `*` of the same length.
Depends on: nothing.
Files: create `crates/kepr-gate/src/mask.rs`. Change `crates/kepr-gate/src/main.rs` (`mod mask;`), `crates/kepr-gate/src/vault.rs` (`Cache::needles`), `crates/kepr-gate/src/dlp.rs` (`MIN_SECRET_LEN` becomes `pub(crate)`), `crates/kepr-gate/src/proxy.rs` (Ok branch of `proxy_request_to` at line 283, and the echo test at line 606), `crates/kepr-gate/src/aws_egress.rs` (Ok branch at line 224) and `crates/kepr-gate/src/recording.rs` (drop `mask()` and the `Masked` carry).
Add:
- `Cache::needles(&self) -> Vec<ZeroString>`: every cached value and every minted token. A value that parses as a JSON object contributes only its string fields `password`, `pending_password`, `secret_access_key`, `session_token` and `private_key`. Skip needles shorter than `dlp::MIN_SECRET_LEN`. Also add each needle's JSON-escaped form (backslash, double quote and slash escaped) when it differs.
- `mask::headers(h: &mut HeaderMap, needles: &[ZeroString])`: mask matched bytes in place, rebuild the `HeaderValue` and call `set_sensitive(true)`.
- `mask::MaskedBody`, implementing `hyper::body::Body<Data = Bytes, Error = hyper::Error>`. For each data frame, mask `carry + frame`, hold back only the longest suffix that is a proper prefix of some needle, and emit the rest. Emit the carry before a trailers frame or at end of stream. `size_hint` is the inner hint plus the carry length. Lengths never change, so `Content-Length` stays right.
- `mask::body(b: Body, needles: Vec<ZeroString>) -> Body`, which returns `b` untouched when there are no needles.
- proxy.rs Ok branch: `let n = state.cache.needles(); mask::headers(&mut p.headers, &n); let b = mask::body(b.boxed(), n);` goes before the recorder and the usage meter. Do the same in aws_egress.rs before `Response::from_parts`.
Steps:
1. Write the tests below. Run them and confirm the echo test and the new ones fail.
2. Add `needles` and `mask.rs`. Wire proxy.rs (330 production lines now; it must stay at or under 350) and aws_egress.rs.
3. Move recording.rs's `a_value_split_across_frames_is_masked` into mask.rs. `http_response` then only tees the frames, which arrive already masked.
Tests:
- proxy.rs `an_upstream_echoing_the_key_is_masked_for_the_agent` (replaces lines 606-625): the agent's body does not contain `REAL`, contains `bad key ` followed by 19 `*`, and has the upstream's length. The recording is masked the same way.
- mask.rs:
  - a value split across two frames is masked;
  - an SSE frame ending in a blank line, with no partial match, is emitted whole in the same poll (SSE is not delayed);
  - a trailing `real-k` is held back, then released unchanged when the next frame is `zzz`;
  - the carry is flushed at end of stream;
  - a needle that is a prefix of another is handled;
  - a JSON-escaped occurrence is masked;
  - a 7-byte value is not masked.
- An upstream response header `x-debug: real-key-…` reaches the agent as `*`s.
- aws_egress.rs: a fake upstream that echoes the secret access key gets it masked.
- vault.rs: `needles` of a `database` record yields its password, but not `user` or `database`.
Out of scope: database and tunnel relays (server-to-agent bytes); responses with a `Content-Encoding` other than identity, which pass unmasked (the gate already asks upstream for identity); values the session never resolved.
Rules: never log, `Debug`-print or put a needle in an error message. Masking buffers are `Zeroizing`. No new path returns a value.
Done when: the standard commands pass.

### T12.02 Gate value cache in secret memory (M) 🔒

Goal: between requests, values the gate caches (resolved credentials and minted GitHub App tokens) live in `memfd_secret` regions, or in `SecretRegion`'s mlocked, non-dumpable fallback, never on the swappable heap.
Depends on: nothing.
Files: create `crates/kepr-gate/src/secret_cell.rs`. Change `crates/kepr-gate/src/main.rs` (`mod secret_cell;`) and `crates/kepr-gate/src/vault.rs`.
Add:
- `pub struct SecretCell { region: Option<SecretRegion>, heap: Option<ZeroString>, len: usize }`, with no `Debug` derive.
- `SecretCell::new(v: &str) -> SecretCell`: copy into `SecretRegion::new(v.len().max(1))`. If that fails, keep a `ZeroString` and print `kepr-gate: secret memory unavailable; caching on the heap` to stderr once per process, with no value or name.
- `reveal(&self) -> ZeroString` (copies out) and `is_secret_backed(&self) -> bool`.
- `Cache.entries` and `Cache.minted` store `SecretCell` instead of `ZeroString`. `cached`, `values` and the minted-token lookups call `reveal()`. `clear()` drops the cells; a region zeroizes and unmaps on drop.
Steps:
1. Write the tests below first.
2. Add `SecretCell`.
3. Switch both maps.
4. Confirm every caller of `values()` and `get()` compiles unchanged (they still receive `ZeroString`).
Tests:
- A round trip for an ASCII value, a multi-byte UTF-8 value, an empty value and a 5000-byte value (PEM-sized, over one page).
- When `kepr_core::secmem::memfd_secret_available()` is true, a new cell is secret-backed (skip otherwise).
- The existing single-flight test still passes (two `get`s, one resolve).
- After a generation change, `values()` is empty.
Out of scope: transient per-request copies (header values, TLS buffers, DLP and mask needles), which hyper and rustls need on the heap; the daemon's cache; relocation timers.
Rules: never `Debug`-print or log a cell; the stderr line carries no value or name; no new path returns a value. Each cell is one page counted against `RLIMIT_MEMLOCK`, and a session caches a few dozen at most.
Done when: the standard commands pass.

### T12.03 Syscall filter for kepr-gate sessions (M) 🔒

Goal: a gate session process can't run programs, ptrace, read another process's memory, load BPF programs or kernel modules, or enter namespaces, even if a parser bug gives an attacker code execution inside it (plan §6.2 seccomp).
Depends on: nothing.
Files: create `crates/kepr-core/src/seccomp.rs`; `crates/kepr-core/src/lib.rs` (`pub mod seccomp;`); `src/cli_sandbox_seccomp.rs` (becomes thin wrappers keeping its public names `denied_syscalls`, `program` and `install`, so callers don't change); `crates/kepr-gate/src/session.rs`.
Add:
- kepr-core `seccomp`, moved verbatim from the CLI module: the AUDIT_ARCH and offset constants, `pub fn program(denied: &[i64]) -> Vec<libc::sock_filter>` and `pub fn install(prog: &[libc::sock_filter]) -> Result<(), &'static str>`.
- `pub fn sandbox_denied() -> Vec<i64>` (today's list).
- `pub fn gate_denied() -> Vec<i64>`: `sandbox_denied()` plus `execve`, `execveat`, `userfaultfd`, `unshare`, `setns`, `mount`, `pivot_root`, `keyctl`, `add_key` and `request_key`.
- Gate: in `session::run()`, directly after `validate_config(&cfg)?` (line 94) and before `Cache::new`, the flusher threads and the tokio runtime: `kepr_core::seccomp::install(&kepr_core::seccomp::program(&kepr_core::seccomp::gate_denied())).map_err(|e| format!("cannot harden the gate: {e}"))?`.
Steps:
1. Write the tests first and confirm they fail.
2. Move the code; the existing sandbox tests must pass unchanged.
3. Wire up the gate.
4. Rules:
   - install before any thread is spawned, because a filter covers only the calling thread and the threads it creates later;
   - never install it in `service::serve`, which starts the session children;
   - use a deny list, not an allowlist;
   - session.rs stays at or under 350 production lines (about 240 now).
Tests:
- kepr-core `program_shape` (moved from the CLI module) and `gate_list_extends_sandbox_list`.
- kepr-core `gate_filter_blocks_exec_and_ptrace`: `fork()`. In the child, install the gate program; `execve("/bin/true")` and `ptrace(PTRACE_TRACEME)` must both fail with EPERM, then `_exit(0)` (otherwise `_exit(1)`). The parent asserts exit status 0.
- `tests/agent_e2e.sh` still passes: the gate serves requests under the filter.
Out of scope: Landlock (T12.04); the user-mode daemon, which starts gdbus, pkcheck, tpm2-tools and kepr-gate.
Done when: the standard commands pass, and so does `tests/agent_e2e.sh`.

### T12.04 Landlock for kepr-gate sessions (M) 🔒

Goal: on kernels with Landlock, a gate session can only read system directories (libraries, NSS and resolver files), can't open anything under $HOME, /root, /run/user or /var/lib/kepr, and can't write files anywhere (plan §6.2 Landlock).
Depends on: nothing.
Files: create `crates/kepr-core/src/landlock.rs`; `crates/kepr-core/src/lib.rs`; `src/cli_sandbox_landlock.rs` (keeps its policy and calls the moved syscall layer); `crates/kepr-gate/src/session.rs`.
Add:
- kepr-core `landlock`, moved from the CLI module: the ABI probe, the access-right constants, and `pub struct Ruleset` with `create(handled: u64) -> Result<Option<Self>, String>` (None means the kernel has no Landlock), `allow(&mut self, path: &Path, access: u64) -> Result<(), String>` and `restrict_self(self) -> Result<(), String>` (sets NO_NEW_PRIVS).
- `pub fn restrict_read_only(paths: &[PathBuf]) -> Result<bool, String>`: handles every filesystem right of the running ABI and grants READ_FILE | READ_DIR beneath each path that exists; returns false when Landlock is unsupported.
- Gate: `fn gate_read_paths() -> Vec<PathBuf>` returns the existing entries among `/etc`, `/usr`, `/lib`, `/lib64`, `/nix/store`, `/proc`, `/sys` and the parent of `fs::canonicalize("/etc/resolv.conf")` (for example /run/systemd/resolve). Apply it in `run()` next to the seccomp hook, before any thread starts. If Landlock is unsupported, print one stderr line and carry on, as the agent sandbox does.
Steps:
1. Write the tests first and confirm they fail.
2. Move the syscall layer; the existing `cli_sandbox_landlock` tests must pass unchanged.
3. Wire up the gate.
4. Rules:
   - never allow `$HOME` or `/run/user`;
   - nothing is writable;
   - no new dependency (kepr-core has no dev-dependencies, so use `std::env::temp_dir()` in tests);
   - new files stay at or under 350 production lines.
Tests:
- kepr-core `read_only_ruleset_denies_other_paths`: create a pid-suffixed file in `std::env::temp_dir()`, then `fork()`. The child restricts to `[/usr]`; reading `/usr/bin/env` must succeed and opening the temp file must fail with EACCES. Skip with 'skip: this kernel has no Landlock'.
- kepr-gate `gate_read_paths_never_include_home`: the result contains `/etc` and no path under `$HOME`.
- The existing sandbox tests pass.
Out of scope: Landlock network rules (ABI 4); the user-mode daemon.
Done when: the standard commands pass, and so does `tests/agent_e2e.sh`. Manual check: `kepr agent run` against a real provider host still resolves DNS.

### T12.05 Basic injection that keeps the client's user name (S) 🔒

Goal: a sealed token can be injected as the password of the client's own `Authorization: Basic` header ("decode, substitute password, re-encode", plan §4.4). git over HTTPS needs this.
Depends on: nothing.
Files: `crates/kepr-core/src/binding.rs`, `crates/kepr-core/src/providers.rs`, `crates/kepr-gate/src/rewrite.rs`, plus any exhaustive `match` on `Inject` that clippy flags (rewrite.rs is the only one with an HTTP meaning).
Add:
- `Inject::BasicPassword` (serde `{"kind":"basic_password"}`).
- `parse_inject("basic-password")`, and list `basic-password` in its error text.
- `providers::BASIC_PASSWORD: &str = ":basic-password"` sentinel, with its arm in `Provider::binding`.
- `rewrite::basic_user(header: &str) -> Option<String>`: the user part of a `Basic` header, next to `basic_placeholder`.
- In `upstream_headers`, `Inject::BasicPassword` produces `Authorization: Basic base64(<user>:<value>)`, marked sensitive. `<user>` is the agent's own Basic user; when it is missing, empty or not UTF-8, use `x-access-token`.
Steps:
1. Write the tests below first.
2. Add the variant and the sentinel.
3. Implement the injection.
4. Run clippy and fix the exhaustive matches it flags.
Tests:
- Agent sends `Basic base64(alice:kepr-ph-…)` with value `REAL`: upstream gets `Basic base64(alice:REAL)`, and the placeholder header is gone.
- Agent sends `Bearer kepr-ph-…`: upstream gets `Basic base64(x-access-token:REAL)`.
- An empty user falls back to `x-access-token`.
- `parse_binding("github.com:basic-password")` validates.
- The serde form is `{"kind":"basic_password"}`.
- The existing `Inject::Basic` test is unchanged.
Out of scope: provider rows or CLI defaults that use the new style (T12.06).
Rules: the value only ever enters the upstream header, marked sensitive. No error carries the value or the decoded header. Older daemons can't parse this binding; that is acceptable, as it was for `sigv4`.
Done when: the standard commands pass.

### T12.06 git over HTTPS preset for github.com (M) 🔒

Goal: inside `kepr agent run`, `git clone|fetch|push https://github.com/...` authenticates with the sealed GitHub token. Setup is just `kepr agent add GH --for github`, and git only ever sees a placeholder.
Depends on: T12.05.
Files: `crates/kepr-core/src/providers.rs` (new row), `src/cli_agent_detect.rs` (`bindings_for`), `src/cli_aws/presets.rs` (`routed`, `shadowed`, `write`), `src/git_helper.rs` (session mode), `src/cli_help.rs` (one line under `agent add`).
Add:
- Provider row `git`: host `github.com`, header `Some(BASIC_PASSWORD)`, prefixes `&[]`, name_hints `&[]` (so `detect` never picks it), keys_url `https://github.com/settings/tokens`, egress_env `&[]`.
- `bindings_for("github")` also returns `Binding { host: "github.com", port: 443, inject: Inject::BasicPassword }`. The api.github.com binding stays Bearer.
- `presets::routed` returns true for `git`.
- When the placeholders contain `git`, `presets::write` returns:
  - `KEPR_PH_GIT_GITHUB_COM=<placeholder>` (`KEPR_PH_` + `git_helper::host_to_kepr_name`);
  - `GIT_CONFIG_COUNT=2`;
  - `GIT_CONFIG_KEY_0` and `GIT_CONFIG_KEY_1` set to `credential.https://github.com.helper`;
  - `GIT_CONFIG_VALUE_0` empty, which resets the helpers from the user's git config;
  - `GIT_CONFIG_VALUE_1` = the absolute path of `git-credential-kepr` next to `current_exe()`, else `kepr`;
  - `GIT_TERMINAL_PROMPT=0`.
- `presets::shadowed` lists those names.
- `git_helper.rs`, when `KEPR_SESSION` is set:
  - `get` answers from the environment only and never contacts the daemon. For `protocol=https` and host H where `KEPR_PH_` + `host_to_kepr_name(H)` is set, it prints `username=x-access-token` and `password=<placeholder>`. Otherwise it prints nothing.
  - `store` and `erase` do nothing.
- `git_helper.rs`, outside a session: `store` refuses a password that starts with `kepr-ph-`. Put the decision in a pure `session_answer(env: &dyn Fn(&str) -> Option<String>, input: &HashMap<String, String>) -> Option<(String, String)>`.
Steps:
1. Write the tests below first.
2. Add the provider row.
3. Add the binding.

6. Add the help line, which says existing sealed GitHub keys need `kepr agent add` again to gain the github.com binding (plan §4.3: bindings change only by re-entering the value).
Tests:

- `bindings_for("github")` returns api.github.com (Bearer) and github.com (BasicPassword).
- `extra_routes` with a sealed credential bound to `github.com:443` adds a `git` route after the harness routes.
- `presets::write` with a `git` placeholder:

- `session_answer`:
  - session and matching host: the two lines;
  - another host: `None`;
  - `protocol=http`: `None`;
  - no session: `None`.
- `store` in a session builds no request; `store` of `kepr-ph-…` outside a session is refused.
Out of scope: gitlab.com, bitbucket.org and self-hosted hosts; git LFS; SSH remotes; request bodies in the gate (T12.07).

### T12.07 Gate: git smart-HTTP bodies on the git route (M) 🔒

Goal: git fetch and clone (gzip-encoded upload-pack requests) and pushes larger than 8 MiB work through the gate's `git` route, which serves only git smart-HTTP paths.
Depends on: T12.06.
Files:
- create `crates/kepr-gate/src/git_http.rs`;
- `crates/kepr-gate/src/main.rs` (`mod git_http;`);
- `crates/kepr-gate/src/egress.rs` (new `To::Git` arm when the intercepted provider is `git`);
- `crates/kepr-gate/src/proxy.rs` (make `build_client` generic over the request body type; no behaviour change);
- `crates/kepr-gate/Cargo.toml` (add `flate2 = { version = "1", default-features = false, features = ["rust_backend"] }`, already in the workspace lock through the root crate).
Add: `git_http::handle(state: Arc<State>, req: Request<Incoming>) -> Result<Response<Body>, Infallible>`, which does the following:
1. Path allowlist. Serve only:
   - `GET /<owner>/<repo>[.git]/info/refs?service=git-upload-pack|git-receive-pack`;
   - `POST /<owner>/<repo>[.git]/git-upload-pack|git-receive-pack`.
   Anything else gets 403 `kepr_blocked` "kepr: the git route serves git smart-HTTP only", and upstream is not contacted.
2. Placeholder check, limits, canary header screen, resolve and `rewrite::upstream_headers`, by calling the same helpers as `proxy_request_to` (no copy of the injection logic).
3. upload-pack: collect the body, up to `MAX_BODY`. If it has `Content-Encoding: gzip`, inflate it into a `Zeroizing<Vec<u8>>` capped at `MAX_BODY` (over the cap: 413). Run `dlp::check` on the inflated bytes with a copy of the parts minus `Content-Encoding`. Forward the original bytes.
4. receive-pack: stream the agent's body upstream without buffering, using a client from the generic `build_client`. Run `dlp::check` on URL and headers with an empty body: pack objects are zlib-deflated, so a byte scan can't see into them. Journal `bytes_out` from a counting wrapper.
5. Response handling is the same as `proxy_request_to`: strip hop-by-hop headers, and mask as T12.01 does.
Steps:
1. Write the tests below first.
2. Make `build_client` generic.
3. Write `git_http.rs`.
4. Add the `To::Git` dispatch in egress.rs.
Tests (fake upstream via `base_override`):
- `info/refs` GET with `Basic base64(x-access-token:<placeholder>)`: upstream sees `Basic base64(x-access-token:REAL)`.
- A gzip upload-pack body that inflates to contain `REAL`: 403 `kepr_blocked`, upstream not contacted.
- A clean gzip upload-pack body is forwarded byte-identical, with `Content-Encoding: gzip`.
- A 20 MiB receive-pack body: upstream receives all 20 MiB, no 413.
- `GET /settings/tokens` on the git route: 403, upstream not contacted.
- A gzip bomb that inflates past `MAX_BODY`: 413.
- A bad placeholder: 401.
Out of scope: git LFS endpoints, the dumb HTTP protocol, other git hosts.
Rules: inflated buffers are `Zeroizing`. No body bytes in the journal or in errors. proxy.rs stays at or under 350 production lines (330 now).
Done when: the standard commands pass.

### T12.08 kepr-core: GCP token sources and the `gcp_token` binding (S) 🔒

Goal: the shared, I/O-free pieces for minting short-lived Google access tokens in the gate: parse a service-account key or an `authorized_user` credential; build the JWT claims and token requests; parse the replies; and a binding that binds only Google API hosts.
Depends on: nothing.
Files: create `crates/kepr-core/src/gcp_token.rs`; modify `crates/kepr-core/src/lib.rs` (mod line), `crates/kepr-core/src/binding.rs` (`Inject::GcpToken` and its validation).
Add:
- `pub enum Source { ServiceAccount { client_email: String, private_key: ZeroString }, AuthorizedUser { client_id: String, client_secret: ZeroString, refresh_token: ZeroString } }` and `pub fn parse_source(json: &str) -> Result<Source, String>`. The `type` field decides the variant. A redacting `Debug`. Errors name the missing field, never a value.
- The file's `token_uri` is ignored. Tokens always come from `pub const TOKEN_URL: &str = "https://oauth2.googleapis.com/token"`.
- `pub fn jwt_claims(client_email: &str, scopes: &[String], now: u64) -> String`: iss; scope, space-joined; aud = TOKEN_URL; iat = now; exp = now + 3600.
- `pub fn jwt_grant_body(assertion: &str) -> ZeroString` and `pub fn refresh_grant_body(s: &Source) -> Option<ZeroString>` (form-urlencoded).
- `pub fn parse_token_reply(body: &[u8]) -> Result<(ZeroString, u64), String>`: the token and `expires_in`.
- `pub fn impersonate_url(target: &str) -> Result<String, String>`: `https://iamcredentials.googleapis.com/v1/projects/-/serviceAccounts/{target}:generateAccessToken`, where `target` must match `[a-z0-9-]+@[a-z0-9-]+.iam.gserviceaccount.com`.
- `pub fn impersonate_body(scopes: &[String], lifetime_secs: u64) -> String` (lifetime at most 3600).
- `pub fn parse_impersonate_reply(body: &[u8]) -> Result<(ZeroString, u64), String>`: the token and the unix expiry, from `expireTime` via a strict UTC parser (`YYYY-MM-DDTHH:MM:SS[.frac]Z`; no new crate).
- `Inject::GcpToken { scopes: Vec<String>, impersonate: Option<String> }`. `validate_binding`: the host ends with `.googleapis.com`, port 443, 1 to 8 scopes each starting `https://www.googleapis.com/auth/`, and `impersonate` passes the email check.
Steps:
1. Write the tests below. Run them and confirm they fail.
2. Write `gcp_token.rs`.
3. Add `Inject::GcpToken` and its arm in `validate_binding`.
Tests:
- A service-account fixture (fake key) and an authorized_user fixture parse.
- A missing `private_key` gives an error containing no field value.
- `token_uri: https://evil.example` in the file is ignored.
- The claims JSON has exactly the five fields.
- Token-reply and impersonation-reply fixtures parse; `2030-01-01T00:00:00Z` → 1893456000.
- A binding on `api.openai.com` is refused, and so is a bad scope.
- serde round trip of `{"kind":"gcp_token",...}`.
Out of scope: signing (T12.09); workload identity federation; `external_account` files.
Security: secrets live in `ZeroString` and Debug output redacts them; no I/O in kepr-core; each file under 350 production lines; MSRV 1.87.
Done when: the standard commands pass.

### T12.09 kepr-gate: mint Google access tokens per session (M) 🔒

Goal: a route whose sealed credential carries `Inject::GcpToken` gets a 1-hour Google access token minted by the gate (a service-account JWT or a refresh-token grant, then optional impersonation), injected as `Authorization: Bearer`. The source credential and the tokens never reach the agent.
Depends on: T12.08.
Files: create `crates/kepr-gate/src/gcp_token.rs`; modify `crates/kepr-gate/src/main.rs` (mod line), `crates/kepr-gate/src/github_app.rs` (`swap` gains an `Inject::GcpToken { .. }` arm calling `crate::gcp_token::token`), `crates/kepr-gate/src/rewrite.rs` (:149, treat `GcpToken` like `GithubApp`: never injected raw).
Add:
- `pub async fn token(state: &State, source_json: &ZeroString, scopes: &[String], impersonate: Option<&str>, now: u64) -> Result<ZeroString, String>`.
- Cache: key = SHA-256 of source ‖ scopes ‖ impersonate, kept next to the GitHub App token cache and reused until 5 min before expiry. Whatever empties the session cache (a store change) ends the token.
- Service account: sign `base64url(header).base64url(claims)` with RS256 (`ring`, key from `github_app::key_pair` on the JSON's PEM), then POST to `TOKEN_URL`.
- authorized_user: POST the refresh grant.
- With `impersonate`: POST `impersonate_url`, carrying the first token as Bearer.
- Replies are capped at 64 KiB and go through `state.client` (same compiled-in roots). Errors carry the HTTP status only.
Steps:
1. Copy the structure of `github_app.rs` (`installation_token`, its cache and its tests).
2. Write the tests below and confirm they fail.
3. Implement.
Tests:
- The JWT header, claims and signature verify with the fixture key's public half.
- The refresh grant body is sent for authorized_user.
- The impersonation call carries the source token and the target email.
- Two requests within the lifetime cause one upstream call; a new token is minted after `expires_in - 300`.
- An upstream 403 → 502 `kepr could not obtain a token`, with no body echoed.
- A cache clear (store change) drops the token.
Out of scope: gcloud's own refresh flow, ID tokens, domain-wide delegation (`sub`).
Security: no token, assertion or key appears in logs, the journal or errors (host and status only, as today). The JWT only ever goes to the pinned `TOKEN_URL`. Each file stays under 350 production lines; no new crate; MSRV 1.87.
Done when: the standard commands pass.

### T12.11 ECDSA P-256 keys for `kepr ssh` (M) 🔒

Goal: `kepr ssh generate NAME --type ecdsa` works, `kepr ssh import` accepts an unencrypted `ecdsa-sha2-nistp256` OpenSSH key, and `kepr ssh-agent` signs with both, sign-only like Ed25519.
Depends on: nothing.
Files:
- create `crates/kepr-core/src/ssh_wire_ecdsa.rs` and add `pub mod ssh_wire_ecdsa;` to `crates/kepr-core/src/lib.rs`;
- `crates/kepr-core/src/ssh_wire.rs`;
- `crates/kepr-core/src/protocol.rs` (`SshGenerate.key_type`);
- create `src/daemon_ssh/ecdsa.rs` as a child module of `src/daemon_ssh.rs`, so `src/daemon.rs` stays untouched;
- `src/daemon_ssh.rs`;
- `src/cli_ssh.rs`;
- the other `SshGenerate` literals, which pass `key_type: None`: `src/daemon_handlers.rs:526`, `src/cli_session/ssh_rotate.rs:222`, `src/daemon_session/ssh_rotate.rs:92`.
Add:
- `ssh_wire_ecdsa` (wire encoding only, no crypto):
  - `ECDSA_P256 = "ecdsa-sha2-nistp256"`;
  - `public_blob(q: &[u8; 65]) -> Vec<u8>`: string type, string `nistp256`, string Q;
  - `signature_blob(r: &[u8; 32], s: &[u8; 32]) -> Vec<u8>`: string type, then string(mpint r ‖ mpint s). An mpint has leading zero bytes stripped and gets a `0x00` prefix when the top bit is set;
  - `public_line(q, comment)`;
  - `blob_type(blob) -> Option<&str>`.
- `ssh_wire::parse_openssh_private` returns `OpensshKey { kind: KeyKind, comment }`, where `KeyKind` is `Ed25519 { seed: Zeroizing<[u8; 32]> }` or `EcdsaP256 { scalar: Zeroizing<[u8; 32]>, q: [u8; 65] }`. Other types are refused with "only ssh-ed25519 and ecdsa-sha2-nistp256 keys are supported".
- `Request::SshGenerate` gains `#[serde(skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none", default)] key_type: Option<String>`: `ed25519` or `ecdsa`; `None` means ed25519; anything else is refused with "unknown key type".
- `daemon_ssh/ecdsa.rs`, using `p256::ecdsa::SigningKey` (RFC 6979, SHA-256) and `Zeroizing` scalar copies:
  - `public_q(scalar: &[u8; 32]) -> Result<[u8; 65], String>`;
  - `sign(scalar: &[u8; 32], data: &[u8]) -> Result<([u8; 32], [u8; 32]), String>`.
- `daemon_ssh.rs`:
  - an ECDSA key's stored value is the base64 of its 32-byte scalar; Ed25519 is unchanged;
  - `sign` picks the algorithm from `ssh_wire_ecdsa::blob_type` of `meta.ssh.public_b64`. No new `.meta` field, so existing keys keep working;
  - `import` checks that the scalar's public point equals the file's Q.
- `cli_ssh.rs`: `generate` takes `--type ed25519|ecdsa`.
Steps:
1. Write the tests below first.
2. Add the wire encoding.
3. Add the parser change.
4. Add the protocol field.
5. Add the daemon signing.
6. Add the CLI flag.
Tests:
- An ECDSA signature blob verifies with `p256::ecdsa::VerifyingKey` built from the stored public blob (pattern: `signature_blob_verifies_with_the_stored_public_key`).
- mpint: an `r` with the top bit set gets `0x00`; an `r` with a leading zero byte is stripped.
- A fixed unencrypted ECDSA OpenSSH fixture (generated once with `ssh-keygen -t ecdsa -N ''`, test-only) parses to the scalar, Q and comment; a Q mismatch is an error.
- `Get` on an ECDSA key is refused with the generic message.
- An existing Ed25519 `.meta` still signs.
- `ssh-keygen -Y verify` accepts an ECDSA signature when `ssh-keygen` is on PATH (skip otherwise).
Out of scope: P-384, P-521, RSA; `kepr ssh rotate` keeping the old key's type (it still generates Ed25519).
Rules: the scalar never leaves the daemon (`Get` refuses; `SshSign` only), and no new path returns it. Audit stays `ssh-sign` with op and name only. `validate_name` runs on every name.
Done when: the standard commands pass.

**Daemon memory and lock triggers**

### T12.21 Master key in secret memory (M) 🔒

Goal: the daemon holds its master key only as two XOR shares in `secmem` regions (memfd_secret pages where the kernel has them, otherwise locked no-dump pages), never as a plain heap array. The key is then never swapped and can't be read through /proc/PID/mem or the kernel direct map.
Depends on: nothing.
Files: `crates/kepr-core/src/secmem.rs`; `src/crypto_inproc.rs`; `src/crypto.rs`; `src/daemon.rs` (line 282 only; add no lines, the file is at its 400-line ceiling); `src/daemon_lock.rs`; `src/daemon_recovery.rs`; `src/daemon_platform/fingerprint.rs` (the type of `unlock_with`'s `unlock` closure only); `src/daemon_store/testkit.rs`; `src/daemon_vault/migrate.rs` (test call); `src/cli_doctor_hardening.rs` (`check_memfd_secret` text).
Add:
- secmem.rs: `pub enum Backing { Auto, Locked }`. `SecretRegion::with_backing(len, Backing) -> Result<Self, String>`: `Locked` skips `map_secret`, and `new(len)` becomes `with_backing(len, Backing::Auto)`. `SplitKey::with_backing(key, Backing)`. `pub enum GuardedKey<const N: usize> { Split(SplitKey<N>), Plain(Zeroizing<[u8; N]>) }` (no `Debug`, no `Clone`) with:
  - `new(key: &[u8; N], b: Backing) -> Self`, which returns `Plain` only when `SplitKey` fails;
  - `with_key<R>(&self, f: impl FnOnce(&[u8; N]) -> R) -> R`;
  - `relocate(&mut self) -> Result<(), String>`, a no-op for `Plain`;
  - `is_split() -> bool` and `addrs() -> Option<(usize, usize)>`.
- crypto_inproc.rs:
  - the field becomes `master_key: Arc<RwLock<Option<GuardedKey<32>>>>`;
  - `new`, `with_store_dir` and `unlock` take `&[u8; 32]`;
  - `key()` is replaced by `fn with_key<R>(&self, f: impl FnOnce(&[u8; 32]) -> Result<R, String>) -> Result<R, String>`, which fails with 'credential store is locked' when there is no key; `encrypt` and `decrypt` call `cipher::seal` / `cipher::open` inside it;
  - `snapshot_key() -> Option<Zeroizing<[u8; 32]>>` is filled inside `with_key`;
  - `pub fn key_addrs(&self) -> Option<(usize, usize)>`.
- crypto.rs:
  - `initialize_inproc_backend(master_key: &[u8; 32], versions)` and `unlock_backend(master_key: &[u8; 32])`;
  - `fn backing() -> Backing`, read from `KEPR_SECRET_MEMORY` (`locked` gives `Locked`, anything else `Auto`);
  - when the key ends up `Plain`, print one stderr line, once per process, guarded by `std::sync::Once`: '[kepr-daemon] secret memory unavailable; master key held in ordinary memory'.
Steps:
1. Write the tests below and confirm they fail.
2. Add the secmem types.
3. Switch `InProcBackend` to `GuardedKey`, and make every caller pass `&[u8; 32]`:
   - daemon.rs:282: `key.as_deref().unwrap_or(&[0u8; 32])`;
   - daemon_lock.rs:82: `unlock_backend(&key)`;
   - daemon_recovery.rs:205;
   - fingerprint.rs: `unlock: impl FnOnce(&[u8; 32]) -> Result<(), String>`, called with `&entry.key`;
   - testkit.rs:32, migrate.rs:203, and the crypto_inproc test at :336.
4. Change the `check_memfd_secret(true)` detail to 'memfd_secret available: kepr's keys are hidden from the kernel direct map; while they exist the kernel refuses hibernation (KEPR_SECRET_MEMORY=locked keeps it)'.
5. Rules:
   - no log line, error text or `Debug` output ever holds key bytes;
   - user-facing text never names the credential backend;
   - crypto_inproc.rs (about 306 production lines now) and secmem.rs (about 262) stay at or under 350;
   - MSRV 1.87.
Tests:
- secmem `guarded_key_round_trips_and_hides_key`: `GuardedKey::new(&[0x42; 32], Backing::Auto)` gives `is_split()`, and `with_key` sees `[0x42; 32]`. Neither share's raw bytes equal the key.
- secmem `locked_backing_skips_memfd_secret`: `SecretRegion::with_backing(16, Backing::Locked)?.is_secret_backed()` is false.
- secmem `plain_key_relocate_is_a_noop`: `relocate()` on a `Plain` key returns Ok, and `with_key` still sees the key.
- crypto_inproc `master_key_is_held_in_split_regions`: `key_addrs()` is `Some((a, b))` with `a != b`, and both addresses fall inside mappings listed in /proc/self/maps. Encrypt and decrypt still round-trip.
- crypto_inproc `lock_drops_the_key`: after `lock()`, `key_addrs()` is None and `encrypt` fails with 'credential store is locked'.
- crypto_inproc `unlock_places_the_key_at_new_addresses`: lock, then `unlock(&[0xAB; 32])`. `key_addrs()` differs from before, and a file written before the lock still decrypts. This covers the plan's 'after each unlock'.
- crypto `snapshot_key_matches_installed_key`: returns the testkit key `[0x5a; 32]` (read-only use of the global backend).
Out of scope: the 60 s relocation and the fingerprint-kept key (T12.22); the gate's value cache and session key (T12.02); vault-image `Keys`; `daemon_cache`.
Done when: the standard commands pass, and so does `tests/agent_e2e.sh`.

### T12.22 Relocate the master key every 60 s (S) 🔒

Goal: every 60 s (plan §6.2), the master key's shares, and the copy kept for the fingerprint window, move to fresh random addresses under a fresh mask, so one memory snapshot doesn't show where the key will be later.
Depends on: T12.21.
Files: `src/crypto_inproc.rs`; `src/crypto.rs`; `src/daemon_platform/fingerprint.rs`; `src/daemon_expiry.rs` (two lines in the sweeper loop).
Add:
- `InProcBackend::relocate(&self) -> Result<(), String>`: takes the write lock and calls `GuardedKey::relocate`; returns Ok when the store is locked.
- `crypto::relocate_key() -> Result<(), String>`: Ok when the backend isn't initialised.
- fingerprint.rs: `Kept.key` becomes `GuardedKey<32>`. `soft_lock` builds it from the snapshot, and `unlock_with` reads it through `with_key`. Add `fn relocate_slot(slot: &Mutex<Option<Kept>>)` and `pub fn relocate()`, which is `relocate_slot(&KEPT)`.
- daemon_expiry.rs, inside the loop of `spawn_sweeper_with_interval` (it already runs every 60 s, `DEFAULT_INTERVAL`):
  - `if let Err(e) = crate::crypto::relocate_key() { eprintln!("[kepr-daemon] key relocation: {e}") }`
  - `crate::daemon_platform::fingerprint::relocate();`
Steps:
1. Write the tests first and confirm they fail.
2. Implement.
3. Rules:
   - don't add a thread;
   - error text carries no key material;
   - daemon_expiry.rs stays at or under 350 production lines (about 284 now).
Tests:
- crypto_inproc `relocate_moves_shares_and_keeps_the_key`: `key_addrs()` differs before and after, and a file encrypted before `relocate()` decrypts after it.
- crypto_inproc `relocate_on_a_locked_store_is_ok`.
- fingerprint `kept_key_survives_relocation`: build a slot holding a `Kept`, call `relocate_slot`, then `unlock_with` hands the original key to `unlock`.
Out of scope: the gate's session key (`crates/kepr-gate/src/proxy.rs:37`), which belongs with T12.02.

Goal: `kepr lock`, `kepr panic`, the idle and sleep locks and the failed-unlock wipe all make every running gate drop its cached values within 250 ms (it can't re-resolve them while the store is locked). In system mode, root's `kepr panic` stops every user's gates (plan P3.5).

### T12.25 Lock when a FIDO2 security key is removed (S) 🔒

Goal: when a FIDO2 security key is unplugged, the user-mode daemon does what suspend does: stop every gate and lock a passphrase-protected store. This applies when the store uses a FIDO2 unlock factor, or when `KEPR_LOCK_ON_KEY_REMOVAL=1`.
Depends on: nothing.

Add:
- `Event::KeyRemoved`.
- `pub fn parse_udev(block: &str) -> Option<Event>`: a block from `udevadm monitor --udev --subsystem-match=hidraw --property` with `ACTION=remove` and `ID_FIDO_TOKEN=1` (set by systemd's 60-fido-id.rules).
- `fn key_watch_enabled(env: Option<&str>, fido2_factor: bool) -> bool`: `0` → off, `1` → on, unset → `fido2_factor`.
- On `KeyRemoved`, call `soft_lock(owner_uid)` and print `[kepr-daemon] KeyRemoved: stopped N gate(s), locked=…` to stderr (no device serials).
Steps:
1. Write the tests below. Run them and confirm they fail.
2. Add `enrolled()` to `fido2_kek.rs`.
3. Add `parse_udev`, `key_watch_enabled` and the watcher thread to `daemon_sleepwatch.rs`.
4. Add the doctor advisory and update the docs.
Tests:

- The truth table of `key_watch_enabled`.
Out of scope: matching one key's serial (any FIDO2 token removal locks); system mode (sleepwatch is user mode only); PIV smart cards.

**Vault image and master-key sealing**

### T12.26 `kepr vault init` defaults to 64 MiB (S)

Goal: `kepr vault init` and the image the daemon creates on first write are 64 MiB (decision 4) unless `--size` says otherwise. `--size` also accepts `64M` and `64MiB`.

Files: `crates/kepr-core/src/protocol_vault.rs`, `src/cli_vault/mod.rs`, `src/daemon_vault/backend.rs`, the test helpers in `src/daemon_vault/admin.rs`, `src/daemon_vault/migrate.rs` and `src/daemon_vault/listing.rs`, `tests/vault_image.rs`, `docs/content/docs/reference/features.mdx` (vault section), `docs/content/docs/agent-vault-plan.mdx` (P4 status), `docs/content/docs/agent-vault-tickets.mdx` (T4.06 status line 'created at 16 MiB').

2. Add the constant, then switch the CLI default, the HELP line ('default 64 MiB') and `parse` to `parse_mib`.
3. Add `create_mib`. Unit-test helpers that rely on create-on-first-write call `.with_create_mib(2)`, so the suite doesn't write 64 MiB of noise per test.
4. In `tests/vault_image.rs`, tests that are not about the default run `kepr vault init --size 4` first.
5. Update the three docs: 16 becomes 64 MiB, and the examples show `--size 64M`.

### T12.27 Vault image: anti-rollback anchor in the header (M) 🔒

Goal: an image can be bound to a monotonic counter kept off the disk, and opening refuses an image older than that counter. Writing back an older `vault.img` together with an older or deleted `cred-versions.json` then no longer resurrects deleted or rotated values.
Depends on: nothing.
Files: `src/daemon_vault/header.rs`, `src/daemon_vault/image.rs` (its two `Header` literals), `src/daemon_vault/records.rs` (`commit_index`), `src/daemon_vault/backend.rs`, `src/daemon_vault/admin.rs` (`create`), `src/daemon_vault/mod.rs` (`pub mod rollback;`), create `src/daemon_vault/rollback.rs`, `src/daemon_vault/journal.rs` (`Header` literals in its tests only).
Add:
- Header body v2 with the same `BODY_LEN`. A new `const INDEX_ROOM: usize = 256` keeps `BODY_LEN = BODY_FIXED + 4 * INDEX_ROOM`, and `MAX_INDEX_SLOTS` becomes 253. The last 12 bytes of the body hold `anchor index u32 LE ‖ anchor value u64 LE`, where index 0 means none. `VERSION = 2`. `decode` accepts v1 (`anchor: None`, up to 256 index slots) and v2 (up to 253). `Header` gains `pub anchor: Option<(u32, u64)>`.
- rollback.rs:
```rust
pub trait EpochCounter: Send + Sync {
    fn index(&self) -> u32;
    /// None: not defined yet.
    fn read(&self) -> Result<Option<u64>, String>;
    /// Defines the counter on first use, adds one, returns the new value.
    fn increment(&self) -> Result<u64, String>;
}
pub fn check_open(h: &Header, c: &dyn EpochCounter) -> Result<(), String>;
/// Unanchored header: anchor at the counter's current value (defining it if needed).
pub fn adopt(h: &mut Header, c: &dyn EpochCounter) -> Result<(), String>;
/// After a durable commit: increment until counter == anchor value; failures are logged, not returned.
pub fn after_commit(h: &Header, c: &dyn EpochCounter);
```
- An `ImageBackend` field `counter: Option<Arc<dyn EpochCounter>>` and `pub fn with_counter(mut self, c: Arc<dyn EpochCounter>) -> Self`. backend.rs has 333 production lines, so it only calls these three functions.
Steps:
1. Write the tests below first.
2. Implement the header v2 encode and decode.
3. In `commit_index`, the new header's `anchor` is `self.header.anchor.map(|(i, v)| (i, v + 1))`.
4. `backend::open` calls `check_open` after `VaultImage::open` and before the `VersionState` check, with these rules:
   - no anchor, counter undefined: ok;
   - no anchor, counter defined: refuse with 'vault image refused: it predates the anti-rollback counter';
   - anchor index differs from `c.index()`: refuse;
   - counter above the anchor value: refuse with 'vault image refused: it is older than the anti-rollback counter (a rolled-back copy?)';
   - counter below the anchor value (a crash between the header write and the increment): increment until equal, at most 1024 times, else refuse.
5. `with_write`: when there is a counter and `anchor == None`, call `adopt` before `f`. After `f` succeeds, call `after_commit`. It logs '[kepr-daemon] vault: anti-rollback counter not advanced; it catches up at the next open' on failure and does not return the error, because the commit is already durable.
6. New images, from `admin::create` and from the create-on-first-write path: `adopt`, then `write_header` + `sync` at epoch 0, so a fresh image is anchored before anyone opens it.
7. Keep the `VersionState` epoch check; it still catches an image-only rollback when no counter is set.
Tests (rollback.rs, with `FakeCounter { value: Mutex<Option<u64>>, fail_next: AtomicBool }`):
- `v1_headers_still_decode_and_v2_round_trips_the_anchor`: build a v1 body by hand.
- `a_rolled_back_copy_is_refused_without_the_state_file`: a backend with a counter sets A; copy `vault.img`; set B; write the copy back. A new `ImageBackend` with an empty `VersionState` and the same counter fails `decrypt` with 'older than the anti-rollback counter'.
- `a_crash_before_the_increment_catches_up`: with `fail_next` set during B's commit, the set returns Ok and the counter is anchor − 1. Reopening succeeds and the counter equals the anchor.
- `an_unanchored_image_is_refused_once_the_counter_exists`.
- `an_unanchored_image_is_adopted_while_the_counter_is_new`.
- `a_new_image_is_anchored_at_once`: create, then reopen; ok, and the counter is unchanged.
- `v2_index_is_capped_at_253_slots`: 'vault index is full'.
- Without a counter, every existing `daemon_vault` test passes unchanged.
Out of scope: the TPM implementation of the counter (T12.30); the per-file store's rollback state. A v1 image with more than 253 index slots opens, but its next commit fails with 'vault index is full'.
Done when: the standard commands pass.

### T12.28 Pad vault records and the index to power-of-two page counts (S) 🔒

Goal: a diff of two images no longer gives a changed record's exact page count (its size to 4 KiB) or the index's size, because records and the index take 1, 2, 4, 8, … pages.
Depends on: T12.27 (cap index padding at its `MAX_INDEX_SLOTS`, 253).
Files: `src/daemon_vault/records.rs`; `docs/content/docs/reference/features.mdx` (Placement bullet).
Add: `fn bucket(pages: usize, cap: usize) -> usize`, equal to `pages.max(1).next_power_of_two().min(cap)`. In `seal_record`, after the real chunks, and in `commit_index`, after the index chunks, seal `bucket - n` extra pages with empty data (`seal_slot(key, &id, s, epoch, &[])`). Place them with the same `alloc.place` call and part numbers n.., and list them in `pages` / `index_slots` and the Merkle leaves. Readers need no change, because an empty page adds nothing to the concatenated payload or index blob (`read_record`, `load_index`). The record format is unchanged.

2. Add `bucket` and the padding loops.
3. Update the doc bullet.

- `index_pages_are_a_power_of_two`.

- `a_3_and_a_4_page_write_change_the_same_number_of_slots`: count differing slots between MemIo snapshots.

### T12.29 Salted, encrypted sessions in the TPM2 sealer (M) 🔒

Goal: with `KEPR_TPM2_PIN` / `KEPR_TPM2_NV_INDEX`, the master key crosses the TPM bus only inside salted sessions with parameter encryption, so sniffing a discrete TPM's LPC/SPI bus at seal or unseal yields only ciphertext.
Depends on: nothing.
Files: `src/daemon_seal/tpm.rs`; create `src/daemon_seal/tpm_blob.rs` (move `Blob`, `put16`, `MAGIC` and `FLAG_*` out of tpm.rs unchanged, which keeps tpm.rs, now at 325 production lines, under 350); create `src/daemon_seal/tpm_session.rs`; `src/daemon_seal/mod.rs` (two `mod` lines); `docs/content/docs/reference/features.mdx` (TPM2 PIN row); `docs/content/docs/agent-vault-plan.mdx` (remove 'salted, encrypted TPM sessions' from the P4 'Not built' list).
Add:
```rust
// tpm_session.rs
pub(super) struct Session<'a, R: Runner> { runner: &'a R, path: String }
impl<'a, R: Runner> Session<'a, R> {
    /// tpm2_startauthsession (--policy-session | --hmac-session) -S PATH -c PRIMARY
    /// (salted with and bound to the primary), then
    /// tpm2_sessionconfig PATH --enable-encrypt --enable-decrypt
    pub fn salted(runner: &'a R, path: String, primary: &str, policy: bool) -> Result<Self, RunError>;
    pub fn path(&self) -> &str;
    /// session:PATH, for -p / -P
    pub fn auth(&self) -> String;
}
impl<R: Runner> Drop for Session<'_, R> { /* tpm2_flushcontext PATH, errors ignored */ }
```
Steps:
1. Move `Blob` into tpm_blob.rs and run the T4.08 tests (no behaviour change).
2. seal: after `primary`, open `Session::salted(.., policy = false)` and add `-P <auth>` to `tpm2_create`. The key on stdin is then parameter-encrypted. The trial session that computes the policy digest stays as it is, since it carries no secret.
3. unseal with flags == 0: run `tpm2_unseal -c key.ctx -p <auth>` over a salted HMAC session, so the response (the key) is encrypted.
4. unseal with flags != 0: `with_policy` starts its policy session through `Session::salted(.., policy = true)`. PolicyPCR and PolicyAuthValue run on that session, and the unseal auth stays `session:PATH[+file:PIN]`.
5. When `tpm2_sessionconfig` is missing, return the existing MISSING text plus '(tpm2-tools 5.0 or newer)'. Any other failure keeps SEAL_FAILED / UNSEAL_FAILED. The tools' stderr stays dropped.
Tests (fake runner in tpm.rs; write these first):
- `unseal_without_policy_uses_a_salted_encrypting_hmac_session` (counter-only config). In call order: `tpm2_startauthsession` with `--hmac-session` and `-c` set to the `-c` path of `tpm2_createprimary`; `tpm2_sessionconfig` on that path with `--enable-encrypt` and `--enable-decrypt`; `tpm2_unseal` with `-p session:<that path>`; `tpm2_flushcontext <that path>`.
- `pin_unseal_runs_its_policy_on_a_salted_session` (PIN + PCRs): the non-trial `--policy-session` start carries `-c <primary>`, and the unseal `-p` is `session:<s>+file:<pin file>`.
- `seal_passes_the_key_over_a_decrypting_session`: `tpm2_create` has `-P session:<s>`, and the `--enable-decrypt` config on `<s>` came before it.
- `every_session_is_flushed_even_when_unseal_fails`: the fake fails `tpm2_unseal`; session starts equal flushes, and the error is 'master-key unseal failed'.
- `old_tools_without_sessionconfig_get_a_clear_error`.
- Every existing T4.08 test passes unchanged; the blob format does not change.
Out of scope: pinning the primary's name against an active bus interposer; the default TPM seal through the credential backend; tss-esapi; swtpm in CI.
Done when: the standard commands pass, and on a host with a TPM (or swtpm), seal, restart and unseal work with `KEPR_TPM2_PIN=1`.

### T12.30 TPM2 NV counter for the vault image epoch (S) 🔒

Goal: `KEPR_TPM2_IMAGE_NV_INDEX=0x01800A12` binds the vault image to its own TPM2 NV counter. An older copy of the whole disk is then refused, and `kepr vault status` shows which rollback guard is active.
Depends on: T12.27. Land it after T12.29, because both edit `tpm.rs`.
Files: create `src/daemon_seal/tpm_counter.rs`; `src/daemon_seal/tpm.rs` (`read_counter` and `bump_counter` move into tpm_counter.rs and the master-key sealer calls them there); `src/daemon_seal/tpm_config.rs`; `src/daemon_seal/mod.rs`; `src/daemon_vault/mod.rs`; `src/daemon_vault/handlers.rs`; `crates/kepr-core/src/protocol_vault.rs`; `src/cli_vault/mod.rs`; `systemd/kepr.service.d/tpm2.conf.example`; `docs/content/docs/reference/features.mdx`.
Add:
```rust
// tpm_counter.rs
pub struct NvCounter<R: Runner> { pub index: u32, pub runner: R }
impl<R: Runner> NvCounter<R> {
    /// tpm2_nvreadpublic fails: None; else tpm2_nvread -C o -s 8 IDX (big-endian)
    pub fn read(&self) -> Result<Option<u64>, String>;
    /// tpm2_nvdefine nt=counter|ownerread|ownerwrite if undefined, tpm2_nvincrement -C o IDX, read
    pub fn increment(&self) -> Result<u64, String>;
}
impl EpochCounter for NvCounter<SystemRunner> { .. }
```
