It errors with 'KEPR_TPM2_IMAGE_NV_INDEX must differ from the master key's counter index' when the value equals `KEPR_TPM2_NV_INDEX`, or equals `DEFAULT_NV_INDEX` while the TPM2 PIN or counter options are on.
- `daemon_seal::image_counter_from_env() -> Result<Option<Arc<dyn EpochCounter>>, String>`.
- In `daemon_vault/mod.rs`, a `fn backend_at(path) -> ImageBackend` attaches the counter. `install_from_env` and `handlers::with_backend` both use it, so `kepr vault init` run against the per-file store also anchors.
- `VaultStatusOk.rollback_guard: Option<String>` (`tpm2` or `state-file`), with `#[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]`. The CLI prints 'rollback guard: TPM2 counter' or 'rollback guard: state file only (a restored copy of the whole disk is not caught)'.
- tpm2.conf.example gets a commented `Environment=KEPR_TPM2_IMAGE_NV_INDEX=0x01800A12`. The docs note the cost: one `tpm2_nvincrement` plus `tpm2_nvread` per image commit.

- `read_of_an_undefined_counter_is_none`.
- `first_increment_defines_then_increments`: args hold `nt=counter|ownerread|ownerwrite` and `0x01800a12`.
- `read_parses_eight_big_endian_bytes`.
- `missing_tools_name_the_package`.
- env: the master key's index is refused, an out-of-range index is refused, and unset gives None.
- An older `VaultStatusOk` JSON without `rollback_guard` still parses.
- The T4.08 tests pass unchanged.
Out of scope: the per-file store; reading the counter over a salted session (the value is not secret).
Done when: the standard commands pass. On a TPM host: set a value, copy `vault.img` and `cred-versions.json`, set again, put both copies back and restart; the daemon refuses the image.

### T12.31 Recovery key for a sealed master key (M) 🔒

Goal: on a store whose master key is sealed (host key, TPM2, KMS, PKCS#11 or keychain) rather than passphrase-protected, `kepr recovery-key enrol` prints 24 words and a QR code once. The daemon keeps the master key wrapped under those words, so a key that stops unsealing can still be recovered.
Depends on: nothing.
Files: `crates/kepr-core/src/protocol_seal.rs`; create `src/daemon_seal/recovery_key.rs`; `src/daemon_seal/mod.rs`; create `src/cli_seal/recovery_key.rs`; `src/cli_seal/mod.rs` (VERBS `recovery-key`, HELP, dispatch); `src/cli_hook.rs` (add `recovery-key` to REVEALING); `crates/kepr-core/src/protocol_session.rs`, `src/daemon_session/status.rs` and `src/cli_status.rs` (`recovery_key: Option<bool>`, shown as 'Recovery key: enrolled' or 'none (kepr recovery-key enrol)'); `AGENTS.md` (one sentence next to the recovery-words note); `docs/content/docs/reference/features.mdx`.
Add:
- `SealRequest::RecoveryKeyEnrol { words: ZeroString, current: Option<ZeroString> }` and `SealResponse::RecoveryKeyEnrolled { fingerprint: String }`. The fingerprint is the master key's, as the kits show. No reply carries key material: the words only ever travel from the CLI to the daemon.
- Daemon: a pure `fn enrol(live: &[u8; 32], existing: Option<&[u8]>, words: &str, current: Option<&str>) -> Result<Vec<u8>, String>` that returns the new envelope, plus `handle_enrol(uid, username, ..)`. The envelope is written to `crate::state_dir().join("master.key.recovery")` as `key_unlock::wrap(live, &recovery_key)` (the shipped Argon2id envelope) with `atomic_file::write(.., 0o600)`.
- CLI `kepr recovery-key enrol [--replace]`: takes 32 bytes from `getrandom::fill` and encodes them with `recovery_words::encode`. With `--replace`, it first reads the current words, masked. After the daemon confirms, it prints `words::grid` and `qr::render` as T4.10 does, then a warning: anyone with these words and the key files can open the store. With `--json`, it prints `{fingerprint, words}`.
Steps:
1. Fences, in this order:
   - owner only (`crate::user_mode() || uid == 0`);
   - no agent gate of any user (`daemon_gate_registry::GATES` non-empty or poisoned gives 'stop running agent sessions first (kepr panic)');
   - not passphrase-protected (else 'this store has a passphrase: use `kepr recovery-kit create --words`');
   - store unlocked (`crypto::snapshot_key`).
2. If an envelope exists, `current` is required and must unwrap it to the live key. Otherwise reply 'a recovery key is already enrolled: `kepr recovery-key enrol --replace`'. Any wrong `current` gets one generic error.
3. Decode `words` (errors name a position, never a word), wrap, write, and audit `recovery-key-enrol` (op only).
Tests (write first):
- `enrol_wraps_the_live_key_under_the_words`: `key_unlock::unwrap(env, decoded)` equals the live key.
- `a_second_enrol_needs_the_current_words`: None or wrong words give Err; the right words give a new envelope that only the new words open.
- `refused_on_a_passphrase_store`.
- `refused_while_any_gate_runs`: use the `track_gate` pattern from the `daemon_vault/handlers.rs` tests.
- `refused_for_non_root_in_system_mode`.
- A protocol round trip, and `Debug` of the request shows no words.
- CLI parse: `enrol` and `enrol --replace` parse; anything else gives usage.
- `kepr hook pre-tool` blocks `kepr recovery-key enrol`.
Out of scope: unlocking with the key (T12.32); Shamir-splitting it; a daemon-generated key (the daemon must not return key material); the T11.13 wipe logic.
Done when: the standard commands pass.

### T12.32 Start locked when the sealed master key won't unseal; unlock with the recovery key (M) 🔒

Goal: when the sealed master key fails to unseal at start-up (PCRs changed after a firmware or kernel update, a changed boot chain, a lost key service) and a recovery key is enrolled, the daemon starts locked instead of exiting. `kepr status` says why, and `kepr recovery-key unlock` opens the store and re-seals the key for the current boot state.
Depends on: T12.31.
Files: create `src/daemon_seal/startup.rs`; `src/daemon.rs`; `src/daemon_seal/mod.rs`; `src/daemon_seal/recovery_key.rs` (`handle_unlock`); `src/daemon_recovery.rs` (`probe` becomes `pub(crate)`); `crates/kepr-core/src/protocol_seal.rs` (`SealRequest::RecoveryKeyUnlock { words: ZeroString }`, answered with `Response::UnlockedOk`); `src/cli_seal/recovery_key.rs` (`unlock`); `src/daemon_session/status.rs` and `src/cli_status.rs` (`key_sealer: unseal-failed`, shown as 'Master key: did not unseal at start-up; run `kepr recovery-key unlock`'); `docs/content/docs/reference/features.mdx`.
In `src/daemon.rs`, replace the bootstrap block at lines 263-274 and the lock at 288-293 with one call to `daemon_seal::startup::bootstrap(user)`. The file is pinned at 400 lines, so its line count must not grow.
Add:
```rust
pub enum Boot { Unlocked(Zeroizing<[u8; 32]>), Locked }
pub fn bootstrap(user: bool) -> Result<Boot, String>;
fn bootstrap_with(sealer: &dyn Sealer, key: &Path, protected: &Path, recovery: &Path) -> Result<Boot, String>;
pub fn unseal_failed() -> bool; // static AtomicBool
```
Steps:
1. `bootstrap_with`:
   - passphrase envelope present: `Locked`, as today, and the sealer is never called;
   - otherwise `master_key::load_or_generate`, and Ok gives `Unlocked`;
   - Err while the key file and the recovery envelope both exist: set the flag, log '[kepr-daemon] the sealed master key did not unseal; unlock with `kepr recovery-key unlock`' (no tool names, no more detail than today), and return `Locked`;
   - any other Err: return Err, and the daemon exits as today.
   Never generate a key while the key file exists.
2. `handle_unlock`:
   - owner only;
   - only while `unseal_failed()` (else 'the master key unsealed; nothing to recover');
   - decode the words and `key_unlock::unwrap` the envelope; any failure gets one generic error, sent after a 1 s pause;
   - `daemon_recovery::probe(&key).0 > 0`, or `daemon_vault::probe_image` opens, else refuse;
   - `crypto::unlock_backend(*key)`;
   - re-seal with `master_key::sealer_from_env(user, &master_key_path())?.seal(&key)` and `atomic_file::write(master_key_path(), &blob, 0o600)`;
   - clear the flag and audit `recovery-unlock`.
   If re-sealing fails, the store stays unlocked for this run and the reply says the next start needs the recovery key again.
Tests (write first; `bootstrap_with` uses failing and counting mock `Sealer`s):
- `a_key_that_will_not_unseal_starts_locked_with_a_recovery_key`.
- `without_a_recovery_key_it_still_exits`.
- `a_missing_key_file_is_generated_as_today`.
- `a_passphrase_store_never_calls_the_sealer`.
- `wrong_words_keep_the_store_locked`.
- `right_words_unlock_and_reseal_once`: the mock seals once and the key file is replaced.
- `refused_when_the_key_unsealed`.
- `refused_for_non_root_in_system_mode`.
- `tests/file_size_limit.rs` passes.
Out of scope: telling an evil maid apart from an update. Re-sealing binds the key to the current boot state, so the docs must say not to unlock on a machine you don't trust. Also out of scope: a re-seal command for keys that still unseal.
Done when: the standard commands pass. On a TPM host or swtpm sealed with `KEPR_TPM2_PCRS=7`, extending PCR 7 leaves the daemon running locked, and `kepr recovery-key unlock` brings it back and survives a restart.

### T12.33 `kepr status` names what seals the master key (S) 🔒

Goal: `kepr status` says whether the master key is TPM2-bound (with PIN, PCRs or counter), host-bound without a TPM, wrapped by a key service, held in the macOS keychain, an owner-only file, or passphrase-protected, so a host that fell back from TPM2 to the host key is visible.
Depends on: nothing.
Files: create `src/daemon_seal/describe.rs`; `src/daemon_seal/mod.rs`; `src/master_key.rs`; `src/daemon_session/status.rs`; `src/cli_status.rs`; `crates/kepr-core/src/protocol_session.rs` (doc comment of `key_sealer` only); `docs/content/docs/reference/features.mdx` (remove the 'passphrase, file or host only' limit).
Add:
- `Sealer::seal_kind(&self) -> Option<String>`, defaulting to `None`. `SystemdCredsSealer` returns its `--with-key` value. It resolves `auto` to `host+tpm2` when `has-tpm2` exits 0, to `host` when it exits non-zero, and to None when it cannot run; this is an internal call and never appears in user text. `load_or_generate` writes the kind to `master.key.seal` next to a newly sealed key (`atomic_file::write`, 0600).
- `describe::describe(blob: &[u8], recorded: Option<&str>) -> String`, which only matches prefixes:
  - `kepr-tpm2-v1\n` plus its flags byte gives `tpm2`, then `+pin` and `+pcrs` as flagged, then `+counter`;
  - `kepr-kek1\n` gives its kind line (`aws-kms`, `gcp-kms`, `azure-kv`, `pkcs11`);
  - `kepr-keychain-v1\n` gives `keychain`;
  - anything else gives `recorded` if that is `host`, `tpm2` or `host+tpm2`, else `host-unrecorded`.
- `key_sealer` in status.rs:
  - passphrase store: `passphrase`, or `passphrase+fido2` for a `kepr_pw2` envelope;
  - user mode with no sealer configured: `file`, **without reading the file** (it holds the raw key);
  - otherwise `describe` on the key file read into a `Zeroizing` buffer, plus `master.key.seal`.
- `sealer_text` in cli_status gives one line per token, for example `host` is 'host key only, no TPM (the credential backend)' and `host-unrecorded` is 'host-bound (TPM use was not recorded for this key)'.
Tests:
- `describe` for each prefix and flag combination.
- A foreign blob with no record gives `host-unrecorded`.
- `seal_kind` resolves `auto` both ways; pass the has-tpm2 answer in as a parameter.
- In user-file mode `key_sealer` returns `file` and never opens the path (point it at an unreadable path).
- `status_lines` renders every token, and the old tokens still render.
Out of scope: changing the default seal; `kepr doctor`.
Done when: the standard commands pass.

**Doctor, packaging and release**

### T12.34 Ship the AppArmor profile in the packages; doctor checks it (S)

Goal: the .deb, .rpm and release tarball install and load `packaging/apparmor/kepr`, which the agent sandbox needs on Ubuntu 24.04+ (decision 3). `kepr doctor` tells a loaded profile apart from a missing one.
Depends on: nothing.
Files: `Cargo.toml` (deb and rpm `assets`, rpm `post_install_script`); `packaging/debian/postinst`; `.github/workflows/release.yml` (tarball step); `src/cli_doctor_hardening.rs` (`check_userns` and `report`); create `tests/packaging_assets.rs`.
Add:
- deb asset `["packaging/apparmor/kepr", "/etc/apparmor.d/kepr", "0644"]`, and the same rpm asset with `config = true`.
- In postinst and the rpm post script: `if command -v apparmor_parser >/dev/null 2>&1 && [ -d /sys/kernel/security/apparmor ]; then apparmor_parser -r -W /etc/apparmor.d/kepr || true; fi`.
- The tarball copies the profile to `packaging/apparmor/kepr` inside the stage, where install.sh looks for it.
- `pub enum Profile { Loaded, Installed, Missing }`, and `check_userns(clone, restrict, max, profile: Profile)`. When restrict is `1`:
  - Loaded gives Good: 'restricted by AppArmor; the kepr profile allows the sandbox';
  - Installed gives Warn: 'kepr AppArmor profile installed but not loaded: sudo apparmor_parser -r /etc/apparmor.d/kepr';
  - Missing gives today's Warn.
- `report()` treats `crate::cli_agent_sandbox::probe(false).is_ok()` as Loaded and an existing `/etc/apparmor.d/kepr` as Installed.
Steps:
1. Write the tests first and confirm they fail.
2. Implement.
3. Rules:
   - postinst never fails the install when AppArmor is absent;
   - no setuid helper.
Tests:
- `userns_profile_states`: the three states with restrict `1`; with restrict `0` the profile state is ignored.
- `tests/packaging_assets.rs`: the Cargo.toml text names `packaging/apparmor/kepr` in both asset tables, and `packaging/debian/postinst` contains `apparmor_parser -r`.
Out of scope: adding kepr-gate, git-credential-kepr and kepr-gate.service to the packages (T12.37).
Done when: the standard commands pass, and `dpkg -c` on the `cargo deb --no-build` output lists `/etc/apparmor.d/kepr`.

### T12.35 `kepr doctor`: hibernation, encrypted swap, Thunderbolt DMA protection (S)

Goal: the hardening section reports three host items from plan §6.2 that it misses today: whether hibernation can write RAM to an unencrypted resume device, whether active swap is encrypted (not only whether it exists), and whether Thunderbolt DMA protection is on.
Depends on: nothing.
Files: create `src/cli_doctor_host.rs`; modify `src/cli_doctor_hardening.rs` (move `check_swap` and its test assertions out, and have `report()` append `crate::cli_doctor_host::report()`) and `src/main.rs` (`mod cli_doctor_host;`).
Add: pure functions returning `cli_doctor_hardening::Finding`, plus one I/O `pub fn report() -> Vec<Finding>`:
- `check_hibernation(state: Option<&str>, resume: Option<&str>, resume_is_crypt: bool)`. Good when `/sys/power/state` has no `disk`, when `/sys/power/resume` is `0:0`, or when the resume device is dm-crypt (`/sys/dev/block/MAJ:MIN/dm/uuid` starts with `CRYPT-`). Otherwise Warn: 'hibernation writes RAM, keys included, to an unencrypted device: encrypt it or disable hibernation'.
- `check_swap(proc_swaps: Option<&str>, is_encrypted: impl Fn(&str) -> bool)`. Good when there is no swap or every device is dm-crypt or zram; otherwise Warn naming the unencrypted devices.
- `check_thunderbolt_dma(values: &[String])`, over `/sys/bus/thunderbolt/devices/domain*/iommu_dma_protection`. With no values, Good 'no Thunderbolt controller'; all `1`, Good; any `0`, Warn 'Thunderbolt DMA protection off: enable it in firmware'.
Steps:
1. Write the tests first, with fixtures.
2. Implement. `report()` resolves `/dev/mapper/*` and `/dev/dm-N` with `fs::canonicalize` and reads `/sys/block/dm-N/dm/uuid`.
3. Rules:
   - the section stays advisory and never changes doctor's exit status;
   - every file stays at or under 350 production lines.
Tests:
- `hibernation_cases`: no `disk` gives Good; `0:0` gives Good; crypt gives Good; a plain device gives Warn.
- `swap_cases`: header only gives Good; `/dev/dm-1` (crypt) and `/dev/zram0` give Good; `/dev/sda2` gives Warn naming it.
- `thunderbolt_cases`: `[]` gives Good; `["1"]` gives Good; `["1","0"]` gives Warn.
Out of scope: TPM2 and Secure Boot (T12.36).
Done when: the standard commands pass.

### T12.36 `kepr doctor`: TPM2, Secure Boot, and an honest lock-on-sleep line (S)

Goal: the hardening section reports TPM2 presence, the SHA-256 PCR bank and Secure Boot state (plan §8 via P3.4). Its lock-on-sleep line stops claiming the store locks when it can't: with no passphrase, or under the system daemon, which has no sleep watcher.
Depends on: T12.35.
Files: `src/cli_doctor_host.rs`; `src/cli_doctor_hardening.rs` (`check_sleep_lock` and `report`); `src/cli_doctor.rs` (one `Request::Status` round trip, passing `mode` and `key_sealer` down).
Add:
- `check_tpm2(version_major: Option<&str>, sha256_bank: bool)`. When `/sys/class/tpm/tpm0/tpm_version_major` is `2`, Good, adding 'sha256 PCR bank' when `/sys/class/tpm/tpm0/pcr-sha256` exists. Otherwise Warn: 'no TPM2: the master key can't be bound to this machine'.
- `check_secure_boot(var: Option<&[u8]>)`, reading `/sys/firmware/efi/efivars/SecureBoot-8be4df61-93ca-11d2-aa0d-00e098032b8c` (4 attribute bytes, then the value). A value of 1 gives Good, 0 gives Warn, and a missing variable gives Warn 'not a UEFI boot, or unknown'.
- `check_sleep_lock(gdbus, system_bus, opted_out, mode: Option<&str>, key_sealer: Option<&str>)`:
  - `Some("system")` gives Warn: 'the system daemon doesn't watch for suspend; root's `kepr panic` locks on demand';
  - user mode with `key_sealer` other than `Some("passphrase")` gives Warn: 'suspend and screen lock stop gates, but the master key stays in memory: set a passphrase (`kepr lock`)';
  - otherwise, today's results.
Steps:
1. Write the tests first and confirm they fail.
2. Implement. If the daemon is unreachable, `mode` and `key_sealer` are None and doctor keeps today's wording.
3. Rules:
   - the section stays advisory;
   - no finding carries a secret;
   - cli_doctor.rs stays at or under 350 production lines (about 263 now).
Tests:
- `tpm2_cases`.
- `secure_boot_cases`: `[6,0,0,0,1]` gives Good; `[6,0,0,0,0]` gives Warn; None gives Warn.
- `sleep_lock_cases`: system gives Warn; user with `file` gives Warn; user with `passphrase`, gdbus and a bus gives Good. The existing three assertions, called with None and None, stay unchanged.
Out of scope: `kepr agent run --strict` (see the doc note in this entry).
Done when: the standard commands pass.

### T12.38 Network-isolated release build (S)

Goal: the release job builds from locked, pre-fetched sources, with a pinned toolchain, no shared cache and no network during `cargo build`. That delivers the plan's "hermetic" SLSA build; the provenance step is unchanged.
Depends on: T12.37 (both edit the build step).
Files: `.github/workflows/release.yml`; create `tests/release_workflow.rs`; `SECURITY.md` ("Verifying a release": one paragraph on how the build runs); `docs/content/docs/agent-vault-plan.mdx` (P7.7 and the P7 status); `docs/content/docs/roadmap.mdx` (the "Still missing" list).
Steps:
1. Write `tests/release_workflow.rs`: text checks on the `build` job of `release.yml`, modelled on `tests/systemd_unit.rs`:
   - (a) no `rust-cache` step;
   - (b) the toolchain input is an exact `X.Y.Z`;
   - (c) a `cargo fetch --locked` step comes before the build;
   - (d) every `cargo build` line has `--frozen` and runs under `unshare --net`;
   - (e) every `cargo install` line has `--locked` and `--version`.
   Confirm it fails on today's file (lines 82, 84, 87, 91-92).
2. Remove `Swatinem/rust-cache` from `build`.
3. Give `dtolnay/rust-toolchain` the input `toolchain: 1.87.0`, which CI already tests (ci.yml:24).
4. Pin `cargo install cargo-deb --locked --version <exact>` and the same for cargo-generate-rpm, using versions current at implementation time.
5. Add a `cargo fetch --locked` step.
6. Build step (`set -euo pipefail`): `sudo unshare --net -- sudo -u "$USER" env PATH="$PATH" HOME="$HOME" CARGO_NET_OFFLINE=true sh -c '! curl -fsS --max-time 5 https://index.crates.io/config.json && cargo build --release --frozen --bins && cargo build --release --frozen -p kepr-gate'`. The curl must fail, which proves the namespace has no network.
7. Update SECURITY.md and the docs.
Tests: `tests/release_workflow.rs` (a)-(e). Then a `workflow_dispatch` run on a throwaway tag in a fork must publish, and `slsa-verifier verify-artifact` must pass on its tarball.
Out of scope: bit-for-bit reproducible builds (SOURCE_DATE_EPOCH, path remapping); a BYOB builder that runs the build itself; arm64; the provenance job.
Rules: actions stay pinned by commit SHA. No secret or token is echoed in the isolated step. `id-token: write` stays on the jobs that need it.
Done when: the four standard commands pass, plus the fork dispatch run.

### T12.39 FIPS seam: HMAC, HKDF and key randomness (M) 🔒

Goal: the daemon's `CipherSuite` also does HMAC-SHA256, HKDF-SHA256 and key/nonce randomness, so a `fips` build runs them in aws-lc-rs. No format changes.
Depends on: nothing.
Files: `src/daemon_eco/cipher.rs`, `src/daemon_eco/crypto_fips.rs`, `src/master_key.rs`, `src/key_unlock.rs`, `src/crypto_inproc.rs`; create `tests/crypto_seam.rs`.
Add (trait methods, plus free functions next to `seal`/`open`/`sha256`):
- `fn hmac_sha256(key: &[u8], parts: &[&[u8]]) -> [u8; 32];`
- `fn hmac_sha256_verify(key: &[u8], parts: &[&[u8]], tag: &[u8]) -> bool;` (full 32-byte tag, constant time)
- `fn hkdf_sha256(salt: Option<&[u8]>, ikm: &[u8], info: &[&[u8]]) -> Zeroizing<[u8; 32]>;` (`None` = RFC 5869 zero salt)
- `fn fill_random(buf: &mut [u8]) -> Result<(), CryptoError>;`
`RustCrypto` uses `hmac`, `hkdf`, `getrandom` (already dependencies). `AwsLc` uses `aws_lc_rs::hmac::{Context, Key, HMAC_SHA256, verify}`, `aws_lc_rs::hkdf::{Salt, HKDF_SHA256}` (`Salt::new(..).extract(ikm).expand(info, HKDF_SHA256)?.fill(..)`) and `aws_lc_rs::rand::fill`.
Steps:
1. Write the tests below in `cipher.rs`. They fail to compile.
2. Implement both suites.
3. Switch key and nonce draws to `cipher::fill_random` at `master_key.rs:36`, `key_unlock.rs:57`, `key_unlock.rs:60` and `crypto_inproc.rs:176`. The error stays "CSPRNG failure" with no detail.
4. Create `tests/crypto_seam.rs`, copying `count_production_loc` from `tests/file_size_limit.rs`. It scans the production part of `src/daemon*.rs`, `src/daemon_*/**/*.rs`, `src/crypto_inproc.rs`, `src/key_unlock.rs`, `src/master_key.rs` and `src/audit_chain.rs` for `aes_gcm::`, `hkdf::`, `Hkdf::`, `hmac::` and `Hmac<`. Allowed: `src/daemon_eco/cipher.rs` and `src/daemon_eco/crypto_fips.rs`. `PENDING` lists the 12 files still to move: `daemon_vault/keys.rs`, `daemon_vault/place.rs`, `daemon_sharing/kem.rs`, `daemon_sharing/link.rs`, `daemon_sharing/emergency_proof.rs`, `daemon_gatex/recfile.rs`, `daemon_seal/fido2_kek.rs`, `daemon_syncx/{device_bundle,folder_bundle,folder_state,quorum_device,sync_state}.rs`. A second test fails when a `PENDING` file no longer matches, so the list only shrinks.
Tests (each runs against `Active`; under `fips` also against `RustCrypto`, like `active_suite_matches_known_answers`):
- `hmac_rfc4231_case2`: key `Jefe`, parts `["what do ya want ", "for nothing?"]` → `5bdcc146bf60754e6a042426089575c75a003f089d2739839dec58b964ec3843`.
- `hmac_verify_rejects_flipped_bit_and_short_tag`.
- `hkdf_rfc5869_case1`: IKM `0x0b`×22, salt `00..0c`, info `f0..f9` passed as two parts → `3cb25f25faacd57a90434f64d0362f2a2d2d0a90cf1a5a4c5db02d56ecc4c5bf`.
- `hkdf_rfc5869_case3_no_salt`: IKM `0x0b`×22, salt `None`, info `[]` → `8da4e775a563c18f715f802a063c5a31b8a11f5c5ee1879ec3454e5f3c738d2d`.
- `fill_random_draws_differ`: two 32-byte draws differ and are not all zero.
- `crypto_seam` passes with `PENDING`.
Out of scope: moving any `PENDING` file; Argon2id, X25519, ML-KEM, Ed25519; any on-disk format; non-key randomness (tokens, ids).
Rules: `CryptoError` stays opaque, with no key, input or library text in errors, logs or `Debug`. Derived keys live in `Zeroizing`. Verification is constant time. No new dependency (aws-lc-rs is already optional behind `fips`). Every file stays under 350 production lines. MSRV 1.87.
Done when: the four standard commands pass, plus `cargo test --features fips --bin kepr-daemon` (CI's `fips` job adds `aws-lc-rs/fips`).

Goal: under `KEPR_STORE=image`, a `fips` build runs everything in aws-lc-rs: image key derivation, slot sealing and opening, slot placement, and the Merkle and journal hashing. Image bytes don't change, so either build opens the other's image.

3. Convert the MACs and KDFs:
   - Each `tag`, `mac` and `bind_mac` returns `[u8; 32]` from `cipher::hmac_sha256(key, &parts)`, with the same parts in the same order (length prefixes included).

   - Each `Hkdf::<Sha256>::new(salt, ikm).expand(info)` → `cipher::hkdf_sha256(salt, ikm, &[info])`.
4. `PENDING` is now empty: delete it, so `crypto_seam` becomes a plain deny test.
5. In `src/cli_eco/crypto.rs`, with `fips: true` the line reads `Crypto: aws-lc-rs (AWS-LC); FIPS mode on (outside the module: Argon2id, X25519, ML-KEM, Ed25519, P-256)`. Update `crypto_line_says_whether_fips_mode_is_on`.

Out of scope: the X25519 key agreement in these files; Argon2id in `fido2_kek.rs`; CLI-side HMAC (`cli_totp.rs`, `cli_platform/kdbx*.rs`).
Rules: MAC checks are constant time. Keys stay in `Zeroizing`. Errors don't change and carry no key material. Every file stays under 350 production lines.

### T12.45 `kepr inject -o` writes only to memory-backed file systems (S) 🔒

Goal: `kepr inject -i T -o OUT` refuses an OUT on a disk-backed file system unless `--allow-disk` is given, so rendered secrets land only on tmpfs or ramfs (plan P5.7).
Depends on: nothing.
Files: create `src/cli_memfs.rs`; modify `src/cli_inject.rs`, `src/cli_extra.rs` (`inject` arm and its usage text), `src/main.rs` (`mod cli_memfs;`), `src/cli_help.rs` (`inject` line), `docs/content/docs/reference/features.mdx` (`inject` row).
Add:
- `cli_memfs::on_memory_fs(f: &std::fs::File) -> bool`: `fstatfs`, TMPFS_MAGIC 0x0102_1994 or RAMFS_MAGIC 0x8584_58f6; keep the `f_type` cast comment.
- `cli_memfs::path_on_memory_fs(p: &Path) -> bool`: `statfs`.
- Delete `on_tmpfs` from `cli_inject.rs`.
- `cmd_inject(input: &str, output: Option<&str>, allow_disk: bool)`, with an inner `fn write_out(out: &Path, allow_disk: bool, render: impl FnOnce() -> Result<Zeroizing<String>, String>) -> Result<(), String>`.
- CLI: `kepr inject -i TEMPLATE [-o OUT [--allow-disk]]`.
Steps:
1. Write the tests below. Run them and confirm they fail.
2. In `write_out`, open OUT with `create_new(true).mode(0o600)` and call `on_memory_fs` on that fd, so there is no check-then-open race. On disk without `allow_disk`: `remove_file(OUT)`, then Err("OUT is on disk: rendered secrets would outlive the process; write under $XDG_RUNTIME_DIR or /dev/shm, use stdout, or pass --allow-disk"). On disk with `allow_disk`: print today's warning.
3. Only then call `render()`, so a refused OUT costs no `get` and no consent, FIDO2 or re-auth prompt. A render error removes OUT.
4. Leave stdout output unchanged.
Tests:
- `memory_fs_true_on_dev_shm` (skip when /dev/shm is not tmpfs).
- `disk_out_refused_before_render`: a path under `env!("CARGO_MANIFEST_DIR")/target` (skip if that is tmpfs); the render closure panics if called; OUT does not exist afterwards.
- `allow_disk_writes_0600`.
- `render_error_leaves_no_file`.
- `existing_out_refused`.
- `cli_extra` parses `--allow-disk`.
Out of scope: a memfd mode (`kepr exec --memfd` / `--pull` already hand values to a command without a file); swap.
Security: errors name the path only, never a value. Values still come from `get`, so sealed, SSH and share-key credentials stay refused. Mode 0600 kept. The file stays under 350 production lines; MSRV 1.87.
Done when: the standard commands pass.

On exit 3, warn "the value passes through a file in /tmp while it is on the clipboard (user namespaces unavailable)" and run plain wl-copy.

2. Add `private_tmp`.
3. Add the internal verb.
4. Switch `cmd_copy` to `wl_copy_argv`.
Tests: `wl_copy_offers_text_plain`; both branches of `wl_copy_argv`; `tests/copy_private_tmp.rs` runs `env!("CARGO_BIN_EXE_kepr") __private-tmp -- sh -c 'stat -f -c %T /tmp'` and expects `tmpfs` (skip on exit 3, when user namespaces are denied); the xclip and xsel argv stay unchanged.
Out of scope: the clipboard-manager hint (T12.47, T12.48); xclip and xsel, which keep the value in memory.

### T12.47 `kepr copy` on Wayland: own the selection and offer the password-manager hint (M) 🔒

Goal: on compositors with a data-control protocol (wlroots compositors, KWin), `kepr copy` offers the value as text plus `x-kde-passwordManagerHint` = `secret`, so clipboard managers that honour the hint don't store it. After SECS it drops the selection, unless another app has taken it.
Depends on: T12.46 (both edit `cmd_copy`).
Files: create `src/cli_clip/mod.rs`, `src/cli_clip/wl_wire.rs`, `src/cli_clip/wayland.rs`; modify `src/main.rs` (`mod cli_clip;`), `src/cli_copy.rs`, `src/cli_extra.rs` (internal verb `__clipboard-serve`), `docs/content/docs/reference/features.mdx` (`copy` row).
Add:
- `cli_clip`: `pub const HINT_MIME: &str = "x-kde-passwordManagerHint"`, `pub const HINT_VALUE: &[u8] = b"secret"`, `pub const TEXT_TYPES: &[&str] = &["text/plain;charset=utf-8", "text/plain", "UTF8_STRING", "TEXT", "STRING"]`, `pub const MAX_VALUE: usize = 1 << 20`.
- `wl_wire`: `enum Arg<'a> { Uint(u32), Int(i32), Str(&'a str), NewId(u32), Object(u32) }`; `fn encode(object: u32, opcode: u16, args: &[Arg]) -> Vec<u8>` (little-endian header: object id, then `size << 16 | opcode`; strings carry a length prefix and a NUL and are padded to 4 bytes); `fn decode(buf: &[u8]) -> Option<(u32, u16, &[u8], usize)>`; `struct Conn` over a `UnixStream`, reading with `recvmsg` and a cmsg buffer for up to 8 SCM_RIGHTS fds, which it keeps in arrival order.
- `wayland::serve(value: &ZeroString, secs: u64, ready: impl FnOnce()) -> Result<(), WlError>`:
  - Connect to `$WAYLAND_DISPLAY` (an absolute path, or a name under `$XDG_RUNTIME_DIR`). Send `wl_display.get_registry`, then `sync`.
  - Bind the first `wl_seat`, and `ext_data_control_manager_v1` (else `zwlr_data_control_manager_v1`; both use the same opcodes: manager `create_data_source`=0, `get_data_device`=1; source `offer`=0, events `send`=0 (mime, fd) and `cancelled`=1; device `set_selection`=0).
  - Offer TEXT_TYPES and HINT_MIME, call `set_selection`, `sync`, then `ready()`.
  - `poll` until the deadline. On `send`, write the value (HINT_VALUE for the hint) and close the fd. On `cancelled`, return Ok: another app owns the clipboard, so there is nothing to clear. At the deadline, `set_selection(0)`, `sync`, return Ok. `wl_display.error` returns Err.
  - `WlError::NoDataControl` when neither manager is advertised.
- `kepr __clipboard-serve` (internal): reads `wayland`, `SECS` and then the value (at most MAX_VALUE, into a `ZeroString`) on stdin. Once the selection is set it writes `ready` to stdout and closes stdout. It exits 3 on `NoDataControl`.
- `cmd_copy` on Wayland spawns `__clipboard-serve` detached (`setsid`, like today's clearer), sends the job on stdin and waits up to 5 s for `ready`. Then it reports success; no hash clearer is needed. On exit 3, an error or no answer, it takes today's wl-copy path and adds `info("this desktop gives clipboard managers no hint; the value is cleared after SECS s")`.
Steps:
1. Write the wire tests.
2. Write `wl_wire`.
3. Write `serve`, tested against a fake compositor.
4. Add `__clipboard-serve`.
5. Wire it into `cmd_copy`.
6. Update the docs.
Tests (write first):
- `encode` of `wl_registry.bind(7, "wl_seat", 8, new_id 5)` equals a fixture.
- `decode` reads a `global` event fixture.
- fds stay in order across two messages.
- A fake compositor on `UnixStream::pair()` advertises a seat and `ext_data_control_manager_v1`. Assert six `offer`s and one `set_selection`. `send("text/plain;charset=utf-8", pipe)` → the pipe reads the value; `send(HINT_MIME, pipe)` → `secret`.
- With a 1 s deadline, the client sends `set_selection(0)`.
- `cancelled` before the deadline → no `set_selection(0)`.
- No data-control global → `NoDataControl`.
- A value over MAX_VALUE is refused.
Out of scope: X11 (T12.48); GNOME/Mutter, which has no data-control protocol (wl-copy fallback); the primary selection.
Security: the value moves only over the stdin pipe and the fds the compositor asks for, and sits in a `ZeroString`. It is never in argv, env, logs or errors. The fetch stays `DaemonFetcher` (`get`), so sealed, SSH and share-key credentials remain refused. No new crate (libc only); each new file under 350 production lines; MSRV 1.87.
Done when: the standard commands pass. Manual check on sway or KDE: after `kepr copy K`, `wl-paste --list-types` lists `x-kde-passwordManagerHint`.

### T12.48 `kepr copy` on X11: own CLIPBOARD and offer the password-manager hint (M) 🔒

Goal: in X11 sessions, `kepr copy` owns the CLIPBOARD selection itself and offers the text targets plus `x-kde-passwordManagerHint` = `secret`. After SECS it gives the selection up, unless another client took it.
Depends on: T12.47 (for `src/cli_clip/mod.rs` and `__clipboard-serve`).
Files: create `src/cli_clip/x11_wire.rs`, `src/cli_clip/x11.rs`; modify `src/cli_clip/mod.rs` (module lines), `src/cli_copy.rs` (X11 branch, with xclip/xsel as the fallback), `src/cli_extra.rs` (`__clipboard-serve` accepts `x11`).
Add:
- `x11_wire::connect(display: &str) -> Result<Conn, String>`:
  - Local displays only (`:N[.S]`, `unix:N`), on `/tmp/.X11-unix/XN`. A TCP display returns Err, which means xclip fallback.
  - Auth: MIT-MAGIC-COOKIE-1 from `$XAUTHORITY` or `~/.Xauthority`. Use an entry of family Local (256) whose hostname matches, or Wild (65535), with the same display number.
  - Parse the setup reply for the resource-id base and mask and the root window.
- Requests: InternAtom (16), CreateWindow (1, InputOnly, 1×1), SetSelectionOwner (22), GetSelectionOwner (23), ChangeProperty (18), SendEvent (25). Events: SelectionRequest (30), SelectionClear (29). Error packets return Err.
- `x11::serve(value, secs, ready)`:
  - Intern CLIPBOARD, TARGETS, every `cli_clip::TEXT_TYPES` name and HINT_MIME.
  - Take CLIPBOARD, confirm with GetSelectionOwner, then call `ready()`.
  - On SelectionRequest:
    - TARGETS → an ATOM list.
    - A text target → ChangeProperty on the requestor's property, format 8, holding the value.
    - HINT_MIME → `secret`.
    - Any other target, or property None → SelectionNotify with property None.
  - On SelectionClear, return Ok. At the deadline, SetSelectionOwner(None) if kepr is still the owner, then Ok.
  - Values over 64 KiB return Err (no INCR), which means xclip fallback.
Tests (write first):
- The Xauthority parser picks the matching entry from a two-entry fixture.
- The setup request bytes with a 16-byte cookie match a fixture.
- Over a fake server on `UnixStream::pair()`:
  - The TARGETS reply lists 7 atoms.
  - A UTF8_STRING request writes the value, then a SelectionNotify.
  - The hint target writes `secret`.
  - An unknown target gets property None.
  - SelectionClear ends `serve` without SetSelectionOwner(None).
  - The deadline sends SetSelectionOwner(None).
Out of scope: INCR transfers, PRIMARY, TCP or forwarded displays.
Security: as in T12.47. The cookie comes from the user's own Xauthority and is never printed. No new crate; MSRV 1.87; each file under 350 production lines.
Done when: the standard commands pass. Manual check under Xvfb with a window manager: after `kepr copy K`, `xclip -o -selection clipboard -t TARGETS` lists `x-kde-passwordManagerHint`.

### T12.50 `kepr rotate --due` and a user timer for the system daemon (S)

Goal: under the system daemon, which never runs user hooks or network rotators, a systemd user timer runs the caller's due scheduled rotations with `kepr rotate --due`.

  - Send `Request::Status`. If `info.mode == Some("user")`, print "the user-mode daemon runs due rotations itself" and return ok, which avoids double runs.

- `cli_help.rs`: add a `rotate --due` line.

### T12.51 Rotate a credential whose check-out ran out (S)

Goal: when a hold lapses without `kepr checkin`, a credential with a rotation schedule is rotated on the next sweep (user-mode daemon), so a value handed out and never returned doesn't stay valid.
Depends on: nothing.
Files: `src/daemon_aws/checkout.rs`, `docs/content/docs/agent-vault-plan.mdx` (§2.3 rotation row, P7 status).
Add:
- `enum Lapse { Live, Cleared, ClearedRotate }`.
- `fn lapse(m: &mut CredMeta, now: u64) -> Lapse`: clears a hold with `until <= now`. When `rotate_every_secs` and `rotate_via` are set, it also sets `rotate_next_at = Some(now)` and returns `ClearedRotate`.
- `sweep` calls `lapse` under the credential lock and writes the meta as today. For each cleared hold it audits `checkout-lapsed` with op and name only. `rotate_sched::sweep` runs the rotation on its next tick.
Steps:
1. Write the tests below.
2. Confirm they fail.
3. Implement `lapse` and use it in `sweep`.
4. Update the docs.
Tests:
- Spent hold with a schedule → hold `None`, `rotate_next_at == Some(now)`, `ClearedRotate`.
- Spent hold without a schedule → `Cleared`, `rotate_next_at` untouched.
- Live hold → `Live`, nothing changes.
- `sweep` on a temp store with one spent, scheduled hold leaves `.meta` with no `checkout` and `rotate_next_at` = now.
Out of scope: the system daemon, which neither sweeps holds nor runs rotations (see T12.50); rotating unscheduled credentials; a per-hold opt-out.
Rules: the audit entry carries op, name, uid and result only. Meta writes go through `expiry::write_meta` under `daemon_credlock`. `checkout.rs` stays under 350 production lines.
Done when: the four standard commands pass.

---

**Found while building P12**

### T12.52 Close inherited descriptors before the harness starts (S) 🔒

Goal: a harness inside a `kepr agent run` sandbox starts with only stdin, stdout, stderr and the descriptors kepr itself hands it. Today every descriptor above 2 that the caller had open passes through, so `kepr agent run claude 3<DIR` gives the harness a handle to a directory the hiding mounts and Landlock were meant to keep from it (found while building T12.20).
Depends on: T12.20.
Files: modify `src/cli_agent_sandbox.rs` (the harness child, after the namespaces and mounts, before Landlock, seccomp and exec), `src/cli_sandbox_sys.rs` (the close helper), `tests/agent_e2e.sh` (the T12.20 cases that use `3<"$MNT"`), `docs/content/docs/reference/features.mdx` (sandbox section).
Add:
- `cli_sandbox_sys::close_inherited(keep: &[RawFd]) -> Result<(), String>`: `close_range(3, ~0, 0)` around the kept descriptors (several calls), or a `/proc/self/fd` walk when `close_range` returns ENOSYS. The kept list is the descriptors kepr set up for the harness (the relay socket for `--isolate-net`, any other kepr-owned fd the child needs).
- Call it in the harness child before Landlock and seccomp, so the filter needs no new syscall; refuse to start (strict) or warn (default) if it fails, like the other sandbox steps.
Steps:
1. Write the tests below. Run them and confirm they fail.
2. Add `close_inherited` and call it.
3. Change the T12.20 e2e cases: assert that `/proc/self/fd/3` is not open in the sandbox when the caller passes `3<"$MNT"`; keep the chrooted-opener and inherited-fd refusals covered by `OpenerGate::check_with` unit tests with the `/proc` reader seam.
Tests:
- unit: open a pipe at a high fd, run `close_inherited` in a forked child with one kept fd, and check from `/proc/self/fd` that only 0–2 and the kept fd remain.
- e2e: `kepr agent run claude --strict-sandbox -- sh -c 'test -e /proc/self/fd/3'` with `3</dev/null` fails; with `--no-sandbox` it succeeds (unchanged).
Out of scope: `--no-sandbox` sessions; an opt-in to keep a descriptor (a later `--keep-fd N` if a harness needs one).
Security: no descriptor the caller opened reaches the sandboxed harness. Nothing is logged but the count closed. Each file stays under 350 production lines; no new crate; MSRV 1.87.
Done when: the standard commands pass and the new `./tests/agent_e2e.sh` cases pass.

### T12.53 Presence check for `kepr lock` and recovery-key enrol on keychain stores (S) 🔒

Goal: with `KEPR_MASTER_KEY_SEAL=keychain` (T10.04), a person at the machine approves `kepr lock` and `kepr recovery-key enrol`. The keychain item is readable only by the daemon binary, but both requests can be sent by any process of the same user, an agent included, and each leaves a way to open the store the keychain does not guard: a passphrase envelope the sender chose, or the master key wrapped under words the sender knows.
Depends on: T12.31, T10.04.
Files: create `src/daemon_seal/presence.rs`; modify `src/daemon_seal/mod.rs`, `src/daemon_seal/recovery_key.rs` (`handle_enrol`), `src/daemon_lock.rs` (`handle_lock`), `docs/content/docs/reference/features.mdx`.
Add:
- `trait Presence { fn confirm(&self, action: &str) -> Result<(), String>; }` and `MacDialog`: `/usr/bin/osascript` `display dialog` naming the action, buttons Deny and Allow, default and cancel Deny, giving up after 60 s. Only `button returned:Allow, gave up:false` with a zero exit is approval; a missing GUI session, a timeout, a cancel or any error is a refusal. On other systems the dialog always refuses.
- `required()` is true when `KEPR_MASTER_KEY_SEAL` is `keychain`; `require_with(required, presence, action)` asks only then, so every other sealer behaves as before.
- `handle_lock` asks after its cheap checks (empty password, already protected) and before it reads the key. `handle_enrol` asks after the fences and before it touches the key or the envelope. Both refusals are audited with the existing `lock` and `recovery-key-enrol` operations; the error never says why a dialog failed.
Steps:
1. Write the `presence.rs` tests below; they fail to compile.
2. Implement the module and the two call sites.
3. Document the prompt and that a refused or unanswered request changes nothing.
Tests:
- a store sealed any other way is never asked;
- a keychain store asks once with the action text and obeys the answer;
- only an explicit Allow in time counts (`gave up:true`, Deny, a non-zero exit and empty output do not);
- without macOS the dialog refuses.
Out of scope: Touch ID or LocalAuthentication (needs a signed helper); prompting for `unlock` or `recovery-key unlock`, which already need a secret; a bypass variable.
Security: the dialog text is fixed; no value, name or word reaches `osascript`. An agent that can drive the dialog through accessibility permissions can still approve it; that permission is the user's to withhold and `kepr doctor` is not yet able to see it.
Done when: the standard commands pass; the macOS e2e run shows a refusal with no answer.

## P13: remaining plan gaps and review follow-ups (2026-10-28)

Everything in P1 to P12 is built. These tickets cover what the plan still
marks missing (🟡 rows in section 2) and the open items of the
[security review](/docs/security-review). They are ordered by priority:
**A** security hardening first, **B** features that close a security
promise, **C** reach (more clients and targets), **D** team and enterprise
scope. Within a group, the first ticket is the one to do first. "Standard
commands" are the four in the build section of `AGENTS.md`, plus
`cargo +1.87.0 clippy --all-targets --all-features -- -D warnings`.

### Priority A: security hardening

### T13.20 Argon2id parameters stored in the envelope (S) 🔒

**Status: shipped** (2026-10-28).

Goal: a passphrase envelope names its own Argon2id cost, so the cost can be raised later without breaking stores written today.
Depends on: nothing.
Files: `src/key_unlock.rs`, `src/daemon_seal/fido2_kek.rs` (uses `derive_key`), `src/daemon_lock.rs`, `tests/vault_image.rs` (only if it pins the magic).
Add:
- A new envelope magic `kepr_pw3\n` followed by `m_kib: u32 BE | t: u32 BE | p: u8 | salt | nonce | ct`. `wrap` writes `kepr_pw3` with the current cost (64 MiB, 3, 1). `unwrap` reads `kepr_pw1` (fixed cost) and `kepr_pw3`, and refuses a `kepr_pw3` cost above 1 GiB, 10 passes or 8 lanes or below 8 MiB, 1 pass, 1 lane (a tampered envelope must not make the daemon allocate gigabytes).
- `kepr unlock` rewrites a `kepr_pw1` envelope as `kepr_pw3` after a successful unlock (atomic write, same passphrase).
Steps:
1. Write the tests below; they fail.
2. Add the format and the bounds. 3. Add the rewrite on unlock.
Tests: a `kepr_pw1` envelope from the old code still opens (pin one); `wrap` output starts with `kepr_pw3\n` and opens; a cost field of 4 GiB, 0 passes and 255 lanes are each refused with the one generic unlock error; unlock of a `pw1` envelope leaves a `pw3` one that opens with the same passphrase.
Out of scope: raising the default cost; the FIDO2 `kepr_pw2` envelope (same change, a later ticket).
Security: the error never says which field was out of range; derivation time is bounded by the caps above.
Done when: the standard commands pass.

### T13.21 Presence check for lock and recovery-key enrol on Linux (S) 🔒

**Status: shipped, opt-in** (2026-10-28): `KEPR_PRESENCE=polkit` makes `lock` and `recovery-key enrol` ask through the new `io.kepr.protect` polkit action (`auth_self`, every time). It is not on by default: a headless host or CI has no polkit agent, so a default would lock those out. The macOS keychain dialog still applies on keychain stores.

Goal: on a store sealed without a passphrase, `kepr lock` and `kepr recovery-key enrol` ask the person at the machine through polkit, as T12.53 does through a macOS dialog, so a process of the same user (an agent) cannot add a way into the store silently.
Depends on: T12.53.
Files: `src/daemon_seal/presence.rs`, `systemd/polkit/io.kepr.policy`, `install.sh`, `docs/content/docs/reference/features.mdx`.
Add:
- `PolkitPresence` implementing `Presence`: `pkcheck --action-id io.kepr.protect --process PID,START --allow-user-interaction` for the caller's pid (the same subject form as `daemon_polkit.rs`), 60 s deadline, any error is a refusal. A new polkit action `io.kepr.protect` (`auth_self_keep` is not allowed: `auth_self`, every time).
- `required()` is true when the store has no passphrase and the sealer is not the owner-only file in an environment with no polkit agent (`KEPR_PRESENCE=off` is not offered; headless hosts set `KEPR_MASTER_KEY_SEAL` or use a passphrase store).
Steps: tests, implementation, policy XML and `install.sh`, docs.
Tests: a fake presence refuses and `lock` / `enrol` change nothing; an approval proceeds; the polkit action ID is in the shipped policy file (`tests/systemd_unit.rs` style text check).
Out of scope: the system daemon's root-only paths; Touch ID.
Done when: the standard commands pass.

### T13.22 Audit chain anchoring (M) 🔒

**Status: shipped** (2026-10-28): `kepr audit anchor --to jsonl:PATH|syslog:udp://HOST[:PORT]` publishes the chain head when it moved; `kepr audit verify --anchor FILE` has the daemon check the log against the anchors (truncated, replaced and rewritten logs each fail). `s3:BUCKET[/PREFIX]` targets write one signed object per anchor (`anchor-<seq>.json`; versioning or object lock on the bucket is what keeps them); verifying against a bucket means downloading its objects into one file first.

Goal: replacing the whole audit log is detectable: the chain head is periodically published somewhere the log's writer cannot rewrite.
Depends on: nothing.
Files: `src/audit_chain.rs`, `src/cli_policy/forward.rs`, `src/cli_policy/tail.rs`, create `src/cli_policy/anchor.rs`.
Add: `kepr audit anchor --to jsonl:PATH | syslog:udp://HOST:PORT | s3:...` writes `{seq, hash, at}` of the chain head with a resume cursor; `kepr audit verify --anchor FILE` fails when the log's entry at `seq` has another hash or the log is shorter than the last anchor.
Steps: tests (a truncated log and a rewritten log both fail verification against an old anchor), then the command.
Out of scope: Rekor or a public log; signing the anchor.
Done when: the standard commands pass.

**Status: shipped, partly** (2026-10-28): `tests/unsafe_comments.rs` lints every `unsafe {` block for a `SAFETY:` comment (all 191 have one; the allowlist is empty and may only shrink). Fuzz targets for `ssh_wire`, `mysql_wire`, `recovery_words`, `shamir`, `http_lite` and the gate messages, with `parser_smoke.rs` running the same entry points on stable. Not done: targets for the bundle header and the KDBX reader (root-crate code the `kepr-core` fuzz crate cannot link); `cargo +nightly fuzz` was not available here, so the targets were not run under libFuzzer.

Goal: every parser of untrusted bytes has a fuzz target, and every `unsafe` block has a one-line `SAFETY:` comment that a test lints for.
Depends on: nothing.
Files: `crates/kepr-core/fuzz/fuzz_targets/`, `tests/unsafe_comments.rs` (create), the files it names.
Add: fuzz targets for `http_lite`, `gate_protocol` frames, `ssh_wire`, `mysql_wire`, `recovery_words::decode`, `shamir` share text, the bundle header and the KDBX reader; `tests/unsafe_comments.rs` fails on any `unsafe {` without a `SAFETY:` comment on the previous or same line (an allowlist for existing violations that only shrinks).
Steps: the lint test first (it fails with the current count), then the comments, then the fuzz targets and a 60 s CI smoke run per target.
Out of scope: rewriting unsafe code.
Done when: the standard commands pass, plus `cargo +nightly fuzz run <each> -- -max_total_time=60`.

### T13.02 Dynamic database users (M) 🔒

**Status: PostgreSQL shipped** (2026-10-28): `kepr agent run --db-user NAME=ROLE[:TTL]` (`gate/pg_ephemeral.rs`): the gate makes `kepr_e_<random>` with a SCRAM verifier and `VALID UNTIL`, grants ROLE, logs the agent in as it, drops it at the end, and sweeps expired `kepr_e_` roles at the next session's start. The sweep is by `VALID UNTIL` in the database, not a daemon-side list. MySQL is not done.

Goal: `kepr agent run --db-user NAME` creates a short-lived PostgreSQL or MySQL login for the session and drops it at the end, so no long-lived database password is released at all.
Depends on: T7.18 (JIT roles).

Add: `DbTarget` gains `ephemeral: Option<Ephemeral { role_template, ttl_secs }>`; the gate, using the sealed admin record, runs `CREATE ROLE kepr_<random> LOGIN PASSWORD '<random>' VALID UNTIL ...` plus the template's grants, logs the agent in as it, and `DROP ROLE` at session end and on a crash sweep at the next start.
Steps: tests with the existing fake servers (create, grant, drop on exit, drop after a gate kill); then the code.
Out of scope: other engines; connection pooling.
Done when: the standard commands pass, plus `./tests/agent_e2e.sh`.

No provider implementation and no `kepr rotate --provider` verb yet: one follow-up ticket per provider.

Goal: `kepr leaked KEY --rotate` and `kepr rotate KEY --provider NAME` replace a sealed key through the provider's own API (GitHub fine-grained tokens, Stripe restricted keys, Anthropic and OpenAI admin keys) without the value ever reaching the CLI.

Steps: split by provider into follow-up tickets first (one provider per ticket); this ticket adds the trait, the daemon commit message and the fake-server harness.
Out of scope: providers without a rotation API.
Done when: the standard commands pass.

Done when: the standard commands pass.

### T13.14 SAML sign-in for device enrolment (L) 🔒

**Status: decided** (2026-10-28): no in-tree SAML. Use an OIDC bridge in front of the SAML IdP; the reasons and the work a direct `--saml` would need are in the SAML guide (`guides/saml-sign-in`). No follow-up ticket until a SAML-only provider is a hard requirement.

Goal: `kepr device enroll --saml` accepts a SAML 2.0 assertion from the identity provider, as `--oidc` does for OIDC.
Depends on: T6.13.
Files: create `src/cli_sharing/saml.rs`.
Add: split first: XML signature verification with exclusive canonicalisation is the risky part and needs its own review and a vetted crate decision; this ticket records the decision (`samael`, or an OIDC bridge in front of the SAML IdP, which is the recommended route).
Tests: none until the decision is made.
Out of scope: SCIM.
Done when: the decision is written in this ticket and the follow-ups exist.

### T13.15 Telnet and the remaining connection types (S)

**Status: shipped** (2026-10-28).

Goal: decide, and write down, that telnet, RDP, VNC and browser isolation stay out of scope.
Depends on: nothing.
Files: `docs/content/docs/agent-vault-plan.mdx`.
Add: the plan's "Connections" row says why: telnet sends the credential in the clear, so a gate that logs in on the agent's behalf would add nothing; RDP, VNC and browser isolation need a hosted gateway.
Done when: the row is marked out of scope (⛔) with the reason and the counts in section 2 are updated.

## Ticket template

Copy this when splitting an L item. A ticket isn't ready for a small model
until every field is filled in with concrete names.

```text
### TX.YY Title (S|M) [🔒 if it touches secrets, auth, crypto or the sandbox]

Goal: one sentence, user-visible outcome.
Depends on: ticket ids, or "nothing".
Files: exact paths to create / modify. Nothing else may change.
Add: exact types, function signatures, protocol variants, CLI syntax.
Steps: numbered, each one checkable.
Tests: named cases with inputs and expected outputs, written first.
Out of scope: what NOT to do in this ticket.
Done when: the standard commands pass (+ any extra command).
```
